After further reflection, our conjecture is as follows: diffusion models may not only adapt to the factorization structure but also to other forms of low-dimensional structure that have not yet been theoretically studied. Even when a density does not exhibit an explicit factorization structure, it may still possess hidden low-dimensional dependencies that diffusion models can effectively capture. Moreover, constructing a distribution completely devoid of any low-dimensional structure is inherently difficult, even in relatively low dimensions (e.g.,
D=5
).

We emphasize that these observations do not contradict classical nonparametric theory. In practice, estimating a five-dimensional function with
100{,}000
samples can be more difficult than estimating a two- or three-dimensional function with only
500
samples, due to the curse of dimensionality.

Furthermore, we conjecture that diffusion models also adapt to non-regular densities, for example, when the Hölder smoothness assumption is violated due to boundary singularities.
In our experiments, for
d=2
and
d=5
, the copula density diverges near the boundary, where the variables
X_{1},\ldots,X_{D}
are highly correlated.
Such violations of regularity assumptions may lead to performance degradation for both BOS and KDE, since these methods rely on explicit pointwise estimation of the target density.

Although not shown in the figures, we also observe that DDPMs substantially outperform BOS and KDE when using alternative marginal distributions, including two-component Beta mixture marginals that vanish at the boundary. In this setting, the training of BOS becomes highly unstable and fails to yield performance improvements even for large sample sizes. In contrast, DDPMs consistently outperform BOS and KDE across these settings, providing empirical evidence of their robustness to a wider class of target distributions.

We also note that BOS becomes highly unstable even in moderately high dimensions (e.g.,
D=30
). This instability arises because BOS directly evaluates the density rather than its logarithm during training, and in high-dimensional settings, the absolute density values become extremely small, leading to numerical underflow. As a result, BOS is not applicable to higher-dimensional cases (
D>30
).

Finally, across all experiments, the performance of DDPMs with fully connected networks is comparable to that of weight-sharing networks. This observation is consistent with the theoretical results reported in
Fan et al. (2025)
. Nevertheless, it is well known that sparse weight-sharing architectures such as U-Net play a crucial role in achieving state-of-the-art performance in practical applications. We therefore regard a deeper investigation into the theoretical advantages of sparse weight-sharing networks as an important direction for future work.

8
Discussion

We have demonstrated that an estimator constructed from the diffusion model is adaptive to the factorization structure and achieves the minimax optimal convergence rates.
In this section, we discuss some future directions related to our work.

Firstly, we believe that our analysis can be extended to other performance measures, such as the Wasserstein distance, following the approach of
Oko et al. (2023)
. Also, our analysis can be extended to high-dimensional settings where the data dimension
D
diverges as the sample size tends to infinity.
In this case, the convergence rate would depend on additional quantities such as
D
and
|\mathcal{I}|
.
An important future task would be to characterize upper bounds for
D
, which might depend on the structure of
p_{0}
, to guarantee statistical consistency.
Although this generalization is a natural extension for statisticians, the techniques required, such as sharp approximation theory, present significant challenges.

Secondly, while we assumed that
P_{0}
possesses a Lebesgue density, this assumption might be eliminated.
For general probability distributions supported on the cube
[0,1]^{D}
, the minimax optimal rate with respect to the Wasserstein distance is
n^{-1/D}
for
D>2
.
If we restrict the class to distributions supported on a
d
-dimensional space (not necessarily a smooth manifold), the optimal rate improves to
n^{-1/d}

(
Weed and Bach, 2019
)
.
It would be interesting to investigate whether an estimator constructed from the diffusion model achieves this optimal rate.
Further structural assumptions could be considered through conditional independence in directed and undirected graphs, which might replace the factorization assumption (
F
) for densities.

Finally, recall that a key component in constructing an optimal estimator is the use of weight-sharing neural networks to approximate functions defined through high-dimensional integrals of the form (
15
).
Recently, physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) have demonstrated remarkable success in modeling solutions to partial differential equations (PDEs)
(
Karniadakis et al., 2021
;
Raissi et al., 2019
)
.
Notably, many solutions to PDEs, such as the heat equation and the Poisson equation, can be expressed as integrals of the form above
(
Courant and Hilbert, 2008
)
.
This suggests that weight-sharing networks could serve as a promising architecture for theoretical analysis of PINNs.

Appendix A
Auxililary Lemmas

This section provides auxiliary lemmas for proving the main theorems.

A.1
Several Bounds Regarding
p_{t}({\bf x})

In this subsection, we present several lemmas that bound
p_{t}({\bf x}),\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x}),
and the derivatives of
p_{t}({\bf x})
.

Lemma 6

(Upper and lower bounds for
p_{t}({\bf x})
)
.

Let
K,\tau_{1}>0
be given and suppose the true density
p_{0}
satisfies that
\tau_{1}\leq p_{0}({\bf x})\leq K
for any
{\bf x}\in[-1,1]^{D}
.
Then, there exists a constant
C_{S,1}=C_{S,1}(D,K,\tau_{1})
such that

\displaystyle C_{S,1}^{-1}\exp\left(-\frac{D\{(\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}-\mu_{t})\vee 0\}^{2}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\leq p_{t}({\bf x})\leq C_{S,1}\exp\left(-\frac{\{(\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}-\mu_{t})\vee 0\}^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)

for every
{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
and
t\geq 0
.

Proof.

This is a re-statement of Lemma A.2 in
Oko et al. (2023)
.
∎

Lemma 7

(Boundedness of score function)
.

Let
K,\tau_{1}>0
be given and suppose the true density
p_{0}
satisfies that
\tau_{1}\leq p_{0}({\bf x})\leq K
for any
{\bf x}\in[-1,1]^{D}
.
Then, there exists a positive constant
C_{S,2}=C_{S,2}(D,K,\tau_{1},\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau})
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x})\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{C_{S,2}}{\sigma_{t}}\left(\frac{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}-\mu_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}\vee 1\right)

for every
{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
and
t\geq 0
.

Proof.

This is a re-statement of Lemma A.3 in
Oko et al. (2023)
.
∎

Lemma 8

(Boundedness of derivatives)
.

Let
K>0
be given and suppose the true density
p_{0}
satisfies that
p_{0}({\bf x})\leq K
for any
{\bf x}\in[-1,1]^{D}
.
For any
{\bf k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{D}
, there exists a positive constant
C_{S,3}=C_{S,3}(D,K,{\bf k},\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau})
such that

\displaystyle\left|({\rm D}^{{\bf k}}p_{t})({\bf x})\right|\leq\frac{C_{S,3}}{\sigma_{t}^{k.}}

for every
{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
and
t\geq 0
.

Proof.

This is a re-statement of Lemma A.3 in
Oko et al. (2023)
, where
k.=\|{\bf k}\|_{1}
.
∎

A.2
Basic Approximation Results for Neural Networks

In this subsection, we present fundamental approximation results for using ReLU networks to approximate elementary functions.
We also define the concatenation and parallelization in weight-sharing networks; see Lemma
13
.

Lemma 9

(Concatenation)
.

Let
K\in{\mathbb{N}},\{d_{1},\ldots,d_{K+1}\}\subseteq{\mathbb{N}}
be given. Consider
L^{(k)}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{\geq 2}
,
s^{(k)},M^{(k)}>0
and
{\bf d}^{(k)}=(d_{1}^{(k)},\ldots,d_{L^{(k)}}^{(k)})^{\top}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{L^{(k)}}
with
d_{1}^{(k)}=d_{k}
and
d_{L^{(k)}}^{(k)}=d_{k+1}
for
k\in[K]
. For any neural networks
f_{1},\ldots,f_{K}
with
f_{k}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L^{(k)},{\bf d}^{(k)},s^{(k)},M^{(k)}),k\in[K]
, there exists a neural network
f\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L,{\bf d},s,M)
with

\displaystyle L=\sum_{k=1}^{K}L^{(k)},\quad\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\max_{k\in[K]}\|{\bf d}^{(k)}\|_{\infty},\quad s\leq 2\sum_{k=1}^{K}s^{(k)},\quad M=\max_{k\in[K]}M^{(k)}

such that
f({\bf x})=(f_{K}\circ\cdots\circ f_{1})({\bf x})
for any
{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}
.

Proof.

This is a re-statement of Remark 13 in
Nakada and Imaizumi (2020)
.
∎

Lemma 10

(Parallelization)
.

Let
K\in{\mathbb{N}}
be given. Consider
L^{(k)}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{\geq 2}
,
s^{(k)},M^{(k)}>0
,
{\bf d}^{(k)}=(d_{1}^{(k)},\ldots,d_{L^{(k)}}^{(k)})^{\top}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{L^{(k)}}
for
k\in[K]
.
For any neural networks
f_{1},\ldots,f_{K}
with

\displaystyle f_{k}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L^{(k)},{\bf d}^{(k)},s^{(k)},M^{(k)}),\quad k\in[K],

there exists a neural network
f\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L,{\bf d},s,M)
with

\displaystyle L=\max_{k\in[K]}L^{(k)},\quad\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|{\bf d}^{(k)}\|_{\infty},

\displaystyle s\leq 2\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(s^{(k)}+Ld_{L^{(k)}}^{(k)}\right),\quad M\leq\left(\max_{k\in[K]}M^{(k)}\right)\vee 1

such that

\displaystyle f({\bf x})=\left(f_{1}({\bf x}^{(1)}),\ldots,f_{K}({\bf x}^{(K)})\right)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{L^{(1)}}^{(1)}+\cdots+d_{L^{(K)}}^{(K)}}

for
{\bf x}=({\bf x}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bf x}^{(K)})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}^{(1)}+\cdots+d_{1}^{(K)}}
. If
L^{(1)}=\cdots=L^{(K)}=\widetilde{L}
with
\widetilde{L}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{\geq 2}
,
(L,{\bf d},s,M)
also satisfies

\displaystyle L=\widetilde{L},\quad\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\|{\bf d}^{(k)}\|_{\infty},\quad s\leq\sum_{k=1}^{(K)}s^{(k)},\quad M\leq\max_{k\in[K]}M^{(k)}.

Proof.

This is a re-statement of Lemma F.3 in
Oko et al. (2023)
.
∎

Lemma 11

(Linear function)
.

Let
W\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}\times d_{1}},{\bf b}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}}
be given with
d_{1},d_{2}\in{\mathbb{N}}
. There exists a neural network
f_{\rm lin}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L,{\bf d},s,M)
with

\displaystyle L=2,\quad{\bf d}=(d_{1},2d_{2},d_{2})^{\top},\quad s=2\|W\|_{0}+2\|{\bf b}\|_{0}+2d_{2},\quad M=\max\{\|W\|_{\infty},\|{\bf b}\|_{\infty},1\}

such that
f_{\rm lin}({\bf x})=W{\bf x}+{\bf b}
for any
{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}
.

Proof.

Note that
W{\bf x}+{\bf b}=\rho(W{\bf x}+{\bf b})-\rho(-W{\bf x}-{\bf b})
for any
{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}
. Let
f_{\rm lin}(\cdot)=W_{2}\rho(W_{1}\cdot+{\bf b}_{1})+{\bf b}_{2}
with

\displaystyle W_{1}=\left(W^{\top},-W^{\top}\right)^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d_{2}\times d_{1}},\quad{\bf b}_{1}=\left({\bf b}^{\top},-{\bf b}^{\top}\right)^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d_{2}},

\displaystyle W_{2}=\left({\mathbb{I}}_{d_{2}},-{\mathbb{I}}_{d_{2}}\right)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}\times 2d_{2}},\quad{\bf b}_{2}=\mathbf{0}_{d_{2}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}},

where
{\mathbb{I}}_{d_{2}}
denotes the
d_{2}\times d_{2}
identity matrix. Then, the assertion is followed by a simple calculation.
∎

Lemma 12

(Identity function)
.

For any
L\geq{\mathbb{N}}_{\geq 2}
and
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
, there exists a neural network
f_{\rm id}^{(m,L)}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L,{\bf d},s,M)
with

\displaystyle{\bf d}=(m,2m,\ldots,2m,m)^{\top},\quad s=2mL,\quad M=1

such that
f_{\rm id}^{(m,L)}({\bf x})={\bf x}
for any
{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
.

Proof.

This is a re-statement of Lemma F.2 in
Oko et al. (2023)
.
∎

The following lemma provides the concatenation and parallelization of two neural networks, where only one network has shared weight.

Lemma 13

(Concatenation and parallelization of weight-sharing networks)
.

Consider the class of weight-sharing networks
\mathcal{F}_{\rm WSNN}(L,{\bf d},s,M,\mathcal{P}_{{\bf m}})
and vanilla feedforward neural networks
\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(\widetilde{L},\widetilde{\bf d},\widetilde{s},\widetilde{M})
.
For any neural networks
f\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm WSNN}(L,{\bf d},s,M,\mathcal{P}_{{\bf m}})
and
\widetilde{f}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(\widetilde{L},\widetilde{\bf d},\widetilde{s},\widetilde{M})
, there exists a neural network
f_{\rm cc}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm WSNN}(L_{\rm cc},{\bf d}_{\rm cc},s_{\rm cc},M_{\rm cc},{\bf m}_{\rm cc},\mathcal{P}_{\rm cc})
with

\displaystyle L_{\rm cc}=L+\widetilde{L},\quad\|{\bf d}_{\rm cc}\|_{\infty}\leq 2(\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}\vee\|\widetilde{\bf d}\|_{\infty}),\quad s_{\rm cc}\leq 2s+2\widetilde{s},\quad M_{\rm cc}=M\vee\widetilde{M},

\|{\bf m}_{\rm cc}\|_{\infty}=\|{\bf m}\|_{\infty}
and the set of permutation matrices
\mathcal{P}_{\rm cc}
such that
f_{\rm cc}({\bf x})=(\widetilde{f}\circ f)({\bf x})
for any
{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}
.
Also, there exists a neural network
f_{\rm pr}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L_{\rm pr},{\bf d}_{\rm pr},s_{\rm pr},M_{\rm pr},{\bf m}_{\rm pr},\mathcal{P}_{\rm pr})
with

\displaystyle L_{\rm pr}=L\vee\widetilde{L},\quad\|{\bf d}_{\rm pr}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}+2\|\widetilde{\bf d}\|_{\infty},

\displaystyle s_{\rm pr}\leq 2s+2\widetilde{s}+2(L\vee\widetilde{L})(d_{L}+\widetilde{d}_{\widetilde{L}}),\quad M_{\rm pr}=\max(M,\widetilde{M},1),

\|{\bf m}_{\rm pr}\|_{\infty}=\|{\bf m}\|_{\infty}
and the set of permutation matrices
\mathcal{P}_{\rm pr}
such that

\displaystyle f_{\rm pr}({\bf x})=\left(f({\bf x}_{1}),\widetilde{f}({\bf x}_{2})\right)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{L}+\widetilde{d}_{\widetilde{L}}}

for any
{\bf x}=({\bf x}_{1},{\bf x}_{2})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}+\widetilde{d}_{1}}
.

Proof.

The first assertion can be easily derived from Remark 13 of
Nakada and Imaizumi (2020)
with

\displaystyle{\bf m}_{\rm cc}=({\bf m},1,\ldots,1)\in{\mathbb{N}}^{L_{\rm cc}-1}\text{\quad and \quad}\mathcal{P}_{\rm cc}=\mathcal{P}\cup\left\{\mathcal{Q}_{l},\mathcal{R}_{l}\right\}_{l\in\{L,\ldots,L_{\rm cc}-1\}},

where

\mathcal{Q}_{l}
and
\mathcal{R}_{l}
are the set of
d_{l}\times d_{l}
and
d_{l+1}\times d_{l+1}
identity matrix, respectively.

For the second part, let
\{W_{l},{\bf b}_{l}\}_{l\in[L]}
and

\{\widetilde{W}_{l},\widetilde{\bf b}_{l}\}_{l\in[\widetilde{L}]}
be the parameter matrices of
f
and
\widetilde{f}
, respectively.
If
L=\widetilde{L}
,
let
\widetilde{\bf d}_{\rm pr}=(d_{1}+\widetilde{d}_{1},\ldots,d_{L+1}+\widetilde{d}_{L+1})
and
{\bf m}_{\rm pr}={\bf m}
.
Also, for each
l\in[L]
, let
\mathcal{Q}_{{\rm pr},l}
and
\mathcal{R}_{{\rm pr},l}
be the set of permutation matrices of the form

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}Q_{l}^{(j)}&\ \mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\ {\mathbb{I}}_{\widetilde{d}_{l}}\end{pmatrix}\text{\quad and \quad}\begin{pmatrix}R_{l}^{(j)}&\ \mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\ {\mathbb{I}}_{\widetilde{d}_{l+1}}\end{pmatrix}

with
j\in[m_{l}]
, respectively.
Then, the assertion follows with

\displaystyle W_{{\rm pr},l}=\begin{pmatrix}W_{l}&\ \mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\ m_{l}^{-1}\widetilde{W}_{l}\end{pmatrix}\text{\quad and \quad}{\bf b}_{{\rm pr},l}=\begin{pmatrix}{\bf b}_{l}\\
m_{l}^{-1}\widetilde{\bf b}_{l}\end{pmatrix},\quad l\in[L-1],

and
\mathcal{P}_{\rm pr}=\left\{\mathcal{Q}_{{\rm pr},l},\mathcal{R}_{{\rm pr},l}\right\}_{l\in[L-1]}
, where
\{W_{{\rm pr},l},{\bf b}_{{\rm pr},l}\}_{l\in[L]}
are the parameter matrices of
f_{\rm pr}
.
If
L=\widetilde{L}+1
, consider a neural network
\overline{{\bf f}}
with
L
-layer and parameter matrices
\{\overline{W}_{l},\overline{{\bf b}}_{l}\}_{l\in[L]}
, where
\overline{W}_{l}=\widetilde{W}_{l},\overline{{\bf b}}_{l}=\widetilde{\bf b}_{l}
for
l\in[\widetilde{L}-1]
, and

\displaystyle\overline{W}_{\widetilde{L}}=\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{W}_{\widetilde{L}}\\
-\widetilde{W}_{\widetilde{L}}\end{pmatrix},\quad\overline{{\bf b}}_{\widetilde{L}}=\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{\bf b}_{\widetilde{L}}\\
-\widetilde{\bf b}_{\widetilde{L}}\end{pmatrix},\quad\overline{W}_{\widetilde{L}+1}=\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbb{I}}_{\widetilde{d}_{\widetilde{L}+1}}\\
-{\mathbb{I}}_{\widetilde{d}_{\widetilde{L}+1}}\end{pmatrix},\quad\overline{{\bf b}}_{\widetilde{L}+1}=\mathbf{0}_{2\widetilde{d}_{\widetilde{L}+1}}.

We then apply the results for the case of parallelization between same layer network.
If
L>\widetilde{L}+1
, consider a weight-sharing neural network
\overline{{\bf f}}={\bf f}_{\rm id}^{(\widetilde{d}_{\widetilde{L}+1},L-\widetilde{L})}\circ\widetilde{\bf f}
with
L
-layer, where
{\bf f}_{\rm id}^{(\widetilde{d}_{\widetilde{L}+1},L-\widetilde{L})}
is the neural network in Lemma
12
.
We then apply the results for the case of parallelization between same layer network.

If
L=\widetilde{L}-1
, consider a weight-sharing neural network
\overline{{\bf f}}
with
\widetilde{L}
-layer, parameter matrices
\{\overline{W}_{l},\overline{{\bf b}}_{l}\}_{l\in[\widetilde{L}]}
with
\overline{W}_{l}=W_{l},\overline{{\bf b}}_{l}={\bf b}_{l}
for
l\in[L-1]
, and

\displaystyle\overline{W}_{L}=\begin{pmatrix}W_{L}\\
-W_{L}\end{pmatrix},\quad\overline{{\bf b}}_{L}=\begin{pmatrix}{\bf b}_{L}\\
-{\bf b}_{L}\end{pmatrix},\quad\overline{W}_{L+1}=\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbb{I}}_{d_{L+1}}\\
-{\mathbb{I}}_{d_{L+1}}\end{pmatrix},\quad\overline{{\bf b}}_{L+1}=\mathbf{0}_{2d_{L+1}},

m_{L}=1
and the set of permutation matrices
\mathcal{P}\cup\{\mathcal{Q}_{L},\mathcal{R}_{L}\}
, where
\mathcal{Q}_{L}
and
\mathcal{R}_{L}
are the set of
d_{L}\times d_{L}
and
2d_{L+1}\times 2d_{L+1}
identity matrix, respectively.
We then apply the results for the case of parallelization between same layer network.
If
L<\widetilde{L}-1
, consider a weight-sharing neural network
\overline{{\bf f}}={\bf f}_{\rm id}^{(d_{L+1},\widetilde{L}-L)}\circ{\bf f}
with
\widetilde{L}
-layer and the set of permutation matrices
\mathcal{P}\cup\{\mathcal{Q}_{l},\mathcal{R}_{l}\}_{L\leq l\leq\widetilde{L}-1}
, where
{\bf f}_{\rm id}^{(d_{L+1},\widetilde{L}-L)}
is the neural network in Lemma
12
and for each
l\in\{L,\ldots,\widetilde{L}-1\}
,
\mathcal{Q}_{l}
and
\mathcal{R}_{l}
are the set of
d_{l}\times d_{l}
and
d_{l+1}\times d_{l+1}
identity matrix, respectively.
We then apply the results for the case of parallelization between same layer network.

∎

Lemma 14

(Multiplication)
.

Let
m\geq 2,C\geq 1,0<\widetilde{\epsilon}\leq 1
be given. For any
\epsilon>0
, there exists a positive constant
C_{N,1}
and a neural network
f_{\rm mult}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L,{\bf d},s,M)
with

\displaystyle L\leq C_{N,1}\log m\{\log(1/\epsilon)+m\log C\},\quad{\bf d}=(m,48m,\ldots,48m,1)^{\top},

\displaystyle s\leq C_{N,1}m\{\log(1/\epsilon)+\log C\},\quad M=C^{m}

such that

\displaystyle\left|f_{\rm mult}(\widetilde{\bf x})-\prod_{i=1}^{m}x_{i}\right|\leq\epsilon+mC^{m-1}\widetilde{\epsilon},\quad\forall{\bf x}\in[-C,C]^{m},\widetilde{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\ {\rm with}\ \|{\bf x}-\widetilde{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq\widetilde{\epsilon},

\|f_{\rm mult}\|_{\infty}\leq C^{m}
and
f_{\rm mult}(\widetilde{\bf x})=0
if
0\in\{\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{m}\}
.

Proof.

This is a re-statement of Lemma F.6 in
Oko et al. (2023)
.
∎

Lemma 15

(Clipping function)
.

Let
\underline{{\bf b}}=(\underline{b}_{1},\ldots,\underline{b}_{m}),\overline{{\bf b}}=(\overline{b}_{1},\ldots,\overline{b}_{m})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
be given with
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
\underline{b}_{i}\leq\overline{b}_{i}
for all
i\in[m]
. Then, there exists a neural network

\displaystyle f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{{\bf b}},\overline{{\bf b}})}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(2,(m,2m,m)^{\top},7m,\|\underline{{\bf b}}\|_{\infty}\vee\|\overline{{\bf b}}\|_{\infty})

such that

\displaystyle f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{{\bf b}},\overline{{\bf b}})}({\bf x})=\left(\overline{b}_{1}\wedge\{x_{1}\vee\underline{b}_{1}\},\ldots,\overline{b}_{m}\wedge\{x_{m}\vee\underline{b}_{m}\}\right)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}

for
{\bf x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
.

Proof.

This is a re-statement of Lemma F.4 in
Oko et al. (2023)
.
∎

Lemma 16

(Logarithm function)
.

For any
0<\epsilon<1/4
, there exists a positive constant
C_{N,2}
and a neural network
f_{\log}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L,{\bf d},s,M)
with

\displaystyle L\leq C_{N,2}\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{2}\log\log(1/\epsilon),\quad\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{N,2}\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{3}

\displaystyle s\leq C_{N,2}\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{5}\log\log(1/\epsilon),\quad M\leq\exp\left(8\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{2}\right)

such that

\displaystyle\left|\log x-f_{\log}(\widetilde{x})\right|\leq\epsilon+\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}|}{\epsilon}

for
x\in[\epsilon,1/\epsilon]
and
\widetilde{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}
.

Proof.

Let
0<\epsilon<1/4,\delta=1/8
and
D_{1}=\lfloor\frac{2\log(1/\epsilon)}{\log(1+\delta)}-1\rfloor+1
. Then,
[\epsilon,1/\epsilon]\subseteq\bigcup_{i=1}^{D_{1}}[\underline{T}_{i},\overline{T}_{i}]
, where
\underline{T}_{i}=(1+\delta)^{i-1}\epsilon
and
\overline{T}_{i}=(1+\delta)^{i+1}\epsilon
for
i\in[D_{1}]
. Let
D_{2}=\lfloor\frac{\log(1/\epsilon)}{\log 2}\rfloor+3
. For any
i\in[D_{1}]
and
x\in[\underline{T}_{i},\overline{T}_{i}]
, Taylor’s theorem yields that

\displaystyle\log x=P_{i}(x)+\frac{(-1)^{D_{2}-1}(x-\underline{T}_{i})^{D_{2}}}{D_{2}\{\underline{T}_{i}+\xi(\overline{T}_{i}-\underline{T}_{i})\}^{D_{2}}}

for a suitable
\xi\in[0,1]
, where

\displaystyle P_{i}(x)=\log\underline{T}_{i}+\frac{x-\underline{T}_{i}}{\underline{T}_{i}}+\sum_{k=2}^{D_{2}-1}\frac{(-1)^{k-1}(x-\underline{T}_{i})^{k}}{k\underline{T}_{i}^{k}}.

Since
x-\underline{T}_{i}\leq\overline{T}_{i}-\underline{T}_{i}=\underline{T}_{i}(\delta^{2}+2\delta)
and
\underline{T}_{i}\leq\underline{T}_{i}+\xi(\overline{T}_{i}-\underline{T}_{i})
, it follows that

\left|\log x-P_{i}(x)\right|\leq\frac{1}{D_{2}}\left(\frac{\overline{T}_{i}-\underline{T}_{i}}{\underline{T}_{i}}\right)^{D_{2}}=\frac{\left(\delta^{2}+2\delta\right)^{D_{2}}}{D_{2}}\leq\frac{2^{-D_{2}}}{D_{2}}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{D_{2}},\quad i\in[D_{1}]

(19)

for
x\in[\underline{T}_{i},\overline{T}_{i}]
.
Let
N_{1}
be a constant in Lemma
14
. For
k\geq 2
, there exists a neural network
f_{\rm mult}^{(k)}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L_{\rm mult}^{(k)},{\bf d}_{\rm mult}^{(k)},s_{\rm mult}^{(k)},M_{\rm mult}^{(k)})
with

\begin{split}&L_{\rm mult}^{(k)}\leq N_{1}(k+D_{2})\log k\{\log(1/\epsilon)+\log D_{2}\},\quad{\bf d}_{\rm mult}^{(k)}=(k,48k,\ldots,48k,1)^{\top},\quad\\
&s_{\rm mult}^{(k)}\leq N_{1}k(k+D_{2})\{\log(1/\epsilon)+\log D_{2}\},\quad M_{\rm mult}^{(k)}=\epsilon^{-k}\end{split}

(20)

such that
|f_{\rm mult}^{(k)}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})-\prod_{i=1}^{k}x_{i}|\leq\epsilon^{D_{2}}/D_{2}
for any
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\in[-\epsilon^{-1},\epsilon^{-1}]
.
For any
k\geq 1
and
i\in[D_{1}]
, Lemma
11
implies that there exists a neural network
f_{\rm lin}^{(i,k)}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(2,(1,2k,k)^{\top},6k,\underline{T}_{i})
such that
f_{\rm lin}^{(i,k)}(x)=(x-\underline{T}_{i},\ldots,x-\underline{T}_{i})^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
for any
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Combining Lemma
9
with the last display, it follows that
f_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}=f_{\rm mult}^{(k)}\circ f_{\rm lin}^{(i,k)}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)},{\bf d}_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)},s_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)},M_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)})
for
i\in[D_{1}],k\geq 2
with

\displaystyle L_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}=L_{\rm mult}^{(k)}+2,\quad\|{\bf d}_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}\|_{\infty}\leq 96k,\quad s_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}=2s_{\rm mult}^{(k)}+12k,\quad M_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}=\underline{T}_{i}\vee M_{\rm mult}^{(k)}

and

\displaystyle\left|f_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}(x)-(x-\underline{T}_{i})^{k}\right|\leq\frac{\epsilon^{D_{2}}}{D_{2}},

for
x\in[\epsilon,\epsilon^{-1}]
.
Consider functions
f_{1},\ldots,f_{D_{1}}:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\displaystyle f_{i}(\cdot)=\log\underline{T}_{i}+\frac{f_{\rm lin}^{(i,1)}(\cdot)}{\underline{T}_{i}}+\sum_{k=2}^{D_{2}-1}\frac{(-1)^{k-1}f_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}(\cdot)}{k\underline{T}_{i}^{k}},\quad i\in[D_{1}].

Since
f_{i}-\log\underline{T}_{i}
is a linear combination of
f_{\rm lin}^{(i,1)},f_{\rm pow}^{(i,2)},\ldots,f_{\rm pow}^{(i,D_{2}-1)}
for
i\in[D_{1}]
, Lemma
9
, Lemma
10
and Lemma
11
implies that
f_{i}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L^{(i)},{\bf d}^{(i)},s^{(i)},M^{(i)})
with

\begin{split}&L^{(i)}\leq L_{\rm pow}^{(i,D_{2}-1)}+2\leq D_{3}D_{2}\log D_{2}\{\log(1/\epsilon)+\log D_{2}\},\\
&\|{\bf d}^{(i)}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\max\left(2\sum_{k=2}^{D_{2}-1}\|{\bf d}_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}\|_{\infty}+4,D_{2}-1\right)\leq D_{3}D_{2}^{2},\\
&s^{(i)}\leq 2\left\{\sum_{k=2}^{D_{2}-1}\left(s_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}+L_{\rm pow}^{(i,D_{2}-1)}+2\right)+L_{\rm pow}^{(i,D_{2}-1)}+8\right\}+2D_{2}+2\\
&\leq D_{3}D_{2}^{3}\log D_{2}\{\log(1/\epsilon)+\log D_{2}\},\\
&M^{(i)}\leq\left(\max_{k\in[D_{2}-1]}M_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}\right)\vee\underline{T}_{i}^{-D_{2}+1}\leq\epsilon^{-D_{2}}\end{split}

(21)

for a large enough constant
D_{3}=D_{3}(N_{1})
.
Then,

\displaystyle\left|P_{i}(x)-f_{i}(x)\right|\leq\sum_{k=2}^{D_{2}-1}\frac{|f_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}(x)-(x-\underline{T}_{i})^{k}|}{k\underline{T}_{i}^{k}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{D_{2}-2}{2\epsilon^{D_{2}-1}}\max_{2\leq k\leq D_{2}-1}\left|f_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}(x)-(x-\underline{T}_{i})^{k}\right|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2},\quad i\in[D_{1}]

for
x\in[\epsilon,\epsilon^{-1}]
, where the first inequality holds because
\underline{T}_{i}\geq\epsilon
.
Combining (
19
) with the last display, we have

\left|\log x-f_{i}(x)\right|\leq\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{D_{2}}\right)\epsilon,\quad i\in[D_{1}]

(22)

fo
x\in[\underline{T}_{i},\overline{T}_{i}]
.
Consider functions
f_{\rm swit}^{(1)},\ldots,f_{\rm swit}^{(D_{1})}:{\mathbb{R}}\to[0,1]
such that

\displaystyle f_{\rm swit}^{(1)}(\cdot)=\frac{1}{\overline{T}_{1}-\underline{T}_{2}}\rho\left(-f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{T}_{2},\overline{T}_{1})}(\cdot)+\overline{T}_{1}\right),

\displaystyle f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(\cdot)=\frac{1}{\overline{T}_{i-1}-\underline{T}_{i}}\rho\left(f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{T}_{i},\overline{T}_{i-1})}(\cdot)-\underline{T}_{i}\right)-\frac{1}{\overline{T}_{i}-\underline{T}_{i+1}}\rho\left(f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{T}_{i+1},\overline{T}_{i})}(\cdot)-\underline{T}_{i+1}\right),

\displaystyle f_{\rm swit}^{(D_{1})}(\cdot)=\frac{1}{\overline{T}_{D_{1}-1}-\underline{T}_{D_{1}}}\rho\left(f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{T}_{D_{1}},\overline{T}_{D_{1}-1})}(\cdot)-\underline{T}_{D_{1}}\right),\quad 2\leq i\leq D_{1}-1,

where
f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{T}_{i},\overline{T}_{i-1})}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(2,(1,2,1)^{\top},7,8\epsilon^{-1})
denotes the neural network in Lemma
15
.
Note that
\sum_{i=1}^{D_{1}}f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(x)=1
for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(x)=0
for
x\in\bigcup_{j=1}^{D_{1}}[\underline{T}_{j},\overline{T}_{j}]\backslash[\underline{T}_{i},\overline{T}_{i}],i\in[D_{1}]
.
Consider a function
f:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that
f(\cdot)=\sum_{i=1}^{D_{1}}f_{\rm mult}^{(2)}(f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(\cdot),f_{i}(\cdot))
. Since
|f_{\rm mult}^{(2)}(x_{1},x_{2})-x_{1}x_{2}|\leq\epsilon^{D_{2}}/D_{2}
for any
x_{1},x_{2}\in[-\epsilon^{-1},\epsilon^{-1}]
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\log x-f(x)\right|\leq\left|\log x-\sum_{i=1}^{D_{1}}f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(x)f_{i}(x)\right|+\frac{D_{1}\epsilon^{D_{2}}}{D_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{i=1}^{D_{1}}f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(x)\left\{\log x-f_{i}(x)\right\}\right|+\frac{D_{1}\epsilon^{D_{2}}}{D_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{D_{1}}f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(x)\left|\log x-f_{i}(x)\right|+\frac{D_{1}\epsilon^{D_{2}}}{D_{2}}\leq\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{D_{2}}\right)\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{D_{1}}f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(x)+\frac{D_{1}\epsilon^{D_{2}}}{D_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1+D_{1}\epsilon^{D_{2}-1}}{D_{2}}\right)\epsilon\leq\epsilon

for
x\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{D_{1}}[\underline{T}_{i},\overline{T}_{i}]
, where the second inequality holds by (
22
).
Combining (
20
) and (
21
) with Lemma
10
and Lemma
9
, we have
f_{\rm mult}^{(2)}(f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(\cdot),f_{i}(\cdot))\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(\widetilde{L}^{(i)},\widetilde{\bf d}^{(i)},\widetilde{s}^{(i)},\widetilde{M}^{(i)})
for
i\in[D_{1}]
with

\displaystyle\widetilde{L}^{(i)}\leq(L^{(i)}\vee 2)+L_{\rm mult}^{(2)}\leq D_{4}D_{2}\log D_{2}\{\log(1/\epsilon)+\log D_{2}\}

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\bf d}^{(i)}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\max\left(2\|{\bf d}^{(i)}\|_{\infty}+4,\|{\bf d}_{\rm mult}^{(2)}\|_{\infty}\right)\leq D_{4}D_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\widetilde{s}^{(i)}\leq 4s^{(i)}+4(L^{(i)}\vee 2)+2s_{\rm mult}^{(2)}+28\leq D_{4}D_{2}^{3}\log D_{2}\{\log(1/\epsilon)+\log D_{2}\}

\displaystyle\widetilde{M}^{(i)}\leq\max\left(8\epsilon^{-1},M^{(i)},M_{\rm mult}^{(2)},1\right)\leq\epsilon^{-D_{2}},

where
D_{4}=D_{4}(D_{3})
is a large enough constant.
Let
f_{\rm clip}^{(\epsilon,\epsilon^{-1})}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(2,(1,2,1)^{\top},7,\epsilon^{-1})
be the nueral network in Lemma
15
.
Since
f
is a linear combination of
f_{\rm mult}^{(2)}(f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(\cdot),f_{i}(\cdot))
for each
i\in[D_{1}]
, Lemma
9
, Lemma
10
and Lemma
11
implies that
f\circ f_{\rm clip}^{(\epsilon,\epsilon^{-1})}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L,{\bf d},s,M)
with

\displaystyle L\leq\max_{i\in[D_{1}]}\widetilde{L}^{(i)}+4\leq D_{5}\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{2}\log\log(1/\epsilon)

\displaystyle\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\max\left(2\sum_{i=1}^{D_{1}}\|\widetilde{\bf d}^{(i)}\|_{\infty},4D_{1}\right)\leq D_{5}\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{3}

\displaystyle s\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{D_{1}}\left(\widetilde{s}^{(i)}+\max_{j\in[D_{1}]}\widetilde{L}^{(j)}\right)+4D_{1}+18\leq D_{5}\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{5}\log\log(1/\epsilon)

\displaystyle M\leq\max_{i\in[D_{1}]}\widetilde{M}^{(i)}\vee\epsilon^{-1}\leq\exp\left(8\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{2}\right)

for large enough constant
D_{5}=D_{5}(D_{4})
.
Note that
|(f\circ f_{\rm clip}^{(\epsilon,\epsilon^{-1})})(\widetilde{x})-\log x|\leq|(f\circ f_{\rm clip}^{(\epsilon,\epsilon^{-1})})(\widetilde{x})-\log(\epsilon^{-1}\wedge\{\widetilde{x}\vee\epsilon\})|+|\log(\epsilon^{-1}\wedge\{\widetilde{x}\vee\epsilon\})-\log x|\leq\epsilon+\epsilon^{-1}|x-\widetilde{x}|
for any
x\in[\epsilon,\epsilon^{-1}]
and
\widetilde{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Then, the assertion follows by re-defining the constant.
∎

Lemma 17

(Negative exponential function)
.

For any
0<\epsilon<2^{-4e+2}
, there exists a positive constant
C_{N,3}
and a neural network
f_{\exp}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L,{\bf d},s,M)
with

\displaystyle L\leq C_{N,3}\log(1/\epsilon)\log\log(1/\epsilon),\quad\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{N,3}\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{3}

\displaystyle s\leq C_{N,3}\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{4},\quad M\leq C_{N,3}\epsilon^{-1}

such that

\displaystyle\left|e^{-x}-f_{\exp}(\widetilde{x})\right|\leq\epsilon+|x-\widetilde{x}|

for any
x\geq 0
and
\widetilde{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}
.

Proof.

Let
0<\epsilon<2^{-4e+2},D_{1}=\lfloor\log(4/\epsilon)\rfloor+1,D_{2}=\lfloor\log(4/\epsilon)/\log 2\rfloor+1
and
\underline{T}_{i}=i-1,\overline{T}_{i}=i+1
for
i\in[D_{1}]
. Then, Taylor’s theorem yields that for any
i\in[D_{1}]
and
x\in[\underline{T}_{i},\overline{T}_{i}]
,

\displaystyle e^{-x}=e^{-\underline{T}_{i}}e^{-(x-\underline{T}_{i})}=e^{-\underline{T}_{i}}\left\{P_{i}(x)+\frac{(-1)^{D_{2}}e^{-\xi(x-\underline{T}_{i})}(x-\underline{T}_{i})^{D_{2}}}{D_{2}!}\right\}

for a suitable
\xi\in[0,1]
, where

\displaystyle P_{i}(x)=1-(x-\underline{T}_{i})+\sum_{k=2}^{D_{2}-1}\frac{(-1)^{k}(x-\underline{T}_{i})^{k}}{k!}.

Since
0\leq x-\underline{T}_{i}\leq 2
and
\underline{T}_{i}\geq 0
, it follows that

\left|e^{-x}-e^{-\underline{T}_{i}}P_{i}(x)\right|\leq\frac{2^{D_{2}}}{D_{2}!}\leq\left(\frac{2e}{D_{2}}\right)^{D_{2}}\leq\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{D_{2}}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4},

(23)

where the second inequality holds because
k!\geq k^{k}e^{-k}
for any
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Let
N_{1}
be a constant in Lemma
14
. For
k\geq 2
, there exists a neural network
f_{\rm mult}^{(k)}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L_{\rm mult}^{(k)},{\bf d}_{\rm mult}^{(k)},s_{\rm mult}^{(k)},M_{\rm mult}^{(k)})
with

\begin{split}&L_{\rm mult}^{(k)}\leq N_{1}\log k\{\log(4D_{2}/\epsilon^{2})+k\log 2\},\quad{\bf d}_{\rm mult}^{(k)}=(k,48k,\ldots,48k,1)^{\top},\quad\\
&s_{\rm mult}^{(k)}\leq N_{1}k\{\log(4D_{2}/\epsilon^{2})+\log 2\},\quad M_{\rm mult}^{(k)}=2^{k}\end{split}

(24)

such that
|f_{\rm mult}^{(k)}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})-\prod_{i=1}^{k}x_{i}|\leq\epsilon^{2}/(4D_{2})
for any
x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\in[-2,2]
.
For any
k\geq 1
and
i\in[D_{1}]
, Lemma
11
implies that there exists a neural network

\displaystyle f_{\rm lin}^{(i,k)}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(2,(1,2k,k)^{\top},6k,\underline{T}_{i})

such that
f_{\rm lin}^{(i,k)}(x)=(x-\underline{T}_{i},\ldots,x-\underline{T}_{i})^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
for any
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Combining Lemma
9
with the last display, it follows that
f_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}=f_{\rm mult}^{(k)}\circ f_{\rm lin}^{(i,k)}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)},{\bf d}_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)},s_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)},M_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)})
for
i\in[D_{1}],k\geq 2
with

\displaystyle L_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}=L_{\rm mult}^{(k)}+2,\quad\|{\bf d}_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}\|_{\infty}\leq 96k,\quad s_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}=2s_{\rm mult}^{(k)}+12k,\quad M_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}=\underline{T}_{i}\vee M_{\rm mult}^{(k)}

and

\displaystyle\left|f_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}(x)-(x-\underline{T}_{i})^{k}\right|\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4D_{2}}

for
x\in[\underline{T}_{i},\overline{T}_{i}]
.
Consider functions
f_{1},\ldots,f_{D_{1}}:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\displaystyle f_{i}(\cdot)=1-f_{\rm lin}^{(i,1)}(\cdot)+\sum_{k=2}^{D_{2}-1}\frac{(-1)^{k}f_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}(\cdot)}{k!},\quad i\in[D_{1}].

Since
f_{i}-1
is a linear combination of
f_{\rm lin}^{(i,1)},f_{\rm pow}^{(i,2)},\ldots,f_{\rm pow}^{(i,D_{2}-1)}
, Lemma
9
, Lemma
10
and Lemma
11
implies that
f_{i}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L^{(i)},{\bf d}^{(i)},s^{(i)},M^{(i)})
with

\begin{split}&L^{(i)}\leq L_{\rm pow}^{(i,D_{2}-1)}+2\leq D_{3}\log D_{2}\{\log(1/\epsilon)+D_{2}\}\\
&\|{\bf d}^{(i)}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\max\left(2\sum_{k=2}^{D_{2}-1}\|{\bf d}_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}\|_{\infty}+4,D_{2}-1\right)\leq D_{3}D_{2}^{2}\\
&s^{(i)}\leq 2\left\{\sum_{k=2}^{D_{2}-1}\left(s_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}+L_{\rm pow}^{(i,D_{2}-1)}+2\right)+L_{\rm pow}^{(i,D_{2}-1)}+8\right\}+2D_{2}+2\\
&\leq D_{3}D_{2}^{2}\{\log(1/\epsilon)+D_{2}\}\\
&M^{(i)}\leq\left(\max_{k\in[D_{2}-1]}M_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}\right)\vee 1\leq(D_{1}+1)\vee 2^{D_{2}-1}\end{split}

(25)

for a large enough constant
D_{3}=D_{3}(N_{1})
.
Then,

\displaystyle\left|P_{i}(x)-f_{i}(x)\right|\leq\sum_{k=2}^{D_{2}-1}\frac{|f_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}(x)-(x-\underline{T}_{i})^{k}|}{k!}

\displaystyle\leq D_{2}\max_{2\leq k\leq D_{2}-1}\left|f_{\rm pow}^{(i,k)}(x)-(x-\underline{T}_{i})^{k}\right|\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4},\quad i\in[D_{1}]

for
x\in[\underline{T}_{i},\overline{T}_{i}]
.
Combining (
23
) with the last display, we have

\left|e^{-x}-e^{-\underline{T}_{i}}f_{i}(x)\right|\leq\left|e^{-x}-e^{-\underline{T}_{i}}P_{i}(x)\right|+\left|P_{i}(x)-f_{i}(x)\right|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2},\quad i\in[D_{1}]

(26)

for
x\in[\underline{T}_{i},\overline{T}_{i}]
, where the first inequality holds because
e^{-\underline{T}_{i}}\leq 1
.
Consider functions
f_{\rm swit}^{(1)},\ldots,f_{\rm swit}^{(D_{1}+1)}:{\mathbb{R}}\to[0,1]
such that

\displaystyle f_{\rm swit}^{(1)}(\cdot)=\frac{1}{\overline{T}_{1}-\underline{T}_{2}}\rho\left(-f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{T}_{2},\overline{T}_{1})}(\cdot)+\overline{T}_{1}\right),

\displaystyle f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(\cdot)=\frac{1}{\overline{T}_{i-1}-\underline{T}_{i}}\rho\left(f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{T}_{i},\overline{T}_{i-1})}(\cdot)-\underline{T}_{i}\right)-\frac{1}{\overline{T}_{i}-\underline{T}_{i+1}}\rho\left(f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{T}_{i+1},\overline{T}_{i})}(\cdot)-\underline{T}_{i+1}\right),

\displaystyle 2\leq i\leq D_{1},

\displaystyle f_{\rm swit}^{(D_{1}+1)}(\cdot)=\frac{1}{\overline{T}_{D_{1}}-\underline{T}_{D_{1}+1}}\rho\left(f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{T}_{D_{1}+1},\overline{T}_{D_{1}})}(\cdot)-\underline{T}_{D_{1}+1}\right),

where
\underline{T}_{D_{1}+1}=D_{1}
and
f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{T}_{i},\overline{T}_{i-1})}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(2,(1,2,1)^{\top},7,D_{1})
denotes the neural network in Lemma
15
.
Note that
\sum_{i=1}^{D_{1}+1}f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(x)=1
for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
, and
f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(x)=0
for
x\in[0,\underline{T}_{i}]\cup[\overline{T}_{i},\infty),i\in[D_{1}]
and
f_{\rm swit}^{(D_{1}+1)}(x)=0
for
x\leq\underline{T}_{D_{1}+1}
.
Consider a function
f:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that
f(\cdot)=\sum_{i=1}^{D_{1}}e^{-\underline{T}_{i}}f_{\rm mult}^{(2)}(f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(\cdot),f_{i}(\cdot))
.
Since
|f_{\rm mult}^{(2)}(x_{1},x_{2})-x_{1}x_{2}|\leq\epsilon^{2}/(4D_{2})
for any
x_{1},x_{2}\in[-2,2]
, we have

\displaystyle\left|e^{-x}-f(x)\right|\leq\left|e^{-x}-\sum_{i=1}^{D_{1}}e^{-\underline{T}_{i}}f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(x)f_{i}(x)\right|+\frac{D_{1}\epsilon^{2}}{4D_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{i=1}^{D_{1}}f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(x)\left\{e^{-x}-e^{-\underline{T}_{i}}f_{i}(x)\right\}+f_{\rm swit}^{(D_{1}+1)}(x)e^{-x}\right|+\frac{D_{1}\epsilon^{2}}{4D_{2}},

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{D_{1}}f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(x)\left|e^{-x}-e^{-\underline{T}_{i}}f_{i}(x)\right|+\frac{\epsilon}{4}+\frac{D_{1}\epsilon^{2}}{4D_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{D_{1}}f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(x)+\frac{\epsilon}{4}+\frac{D_{1}\epsilon^{2}}{4D_{2}}\leq\left(\frac{3}{4}+\frac{(D_{1}+1)\epsilon}{4D_{2}}\right)\epsilon\leq\epsilon

for
x\in[0,\infty)
, where the second inequality holds because
|f_{\rm swit}^{(D_{1}+1)}(x)e^{-x}|\leq e^{-x}\leq\epsilon/4
for
x\geq\underline{T}_{D_{1}+1}
and the third inequality holds by (
26
).
Combining (
24
) and (
25
) with Lemma
10
and Lemma
9
, we have
f_{\rm mult}^{(2)}(f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(\cdot),f_{i}(\cdot))\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(\widetilde{L}^{(i)},\widetilde{\bf d}^{(i)},\widetilde{s}^{(i)},\widetilde{M}^{(i)})
for
i\in[D_{1}]
with

\displaystyle\widetilde{L}^{(i)}\leq(L^{(i)}\vee 2)+L_{\rm mult}^{(2)}\leq D_{4}\log D_{2}\{\log(1/\epsilon)+\log D_{2}\}

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\bf d}^{(i)}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\max\left(2\|{\bf d}^{(i)}\|_{\infty}+4,\|{\bf d}_{\rm mult}^{(2)}\|_{\infty}\right)\leq D_{4}D_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\widetilde{s}^{(i)}\leq 4s^{(i)}+4(L^{(i)}\vee 2)+2s_{\rm mult}^{(2)}+28\leq D_{4}D_{2}^{2}\{\log(1/\epsilon)+D_{2}\}

\displaystyle\widetilde{M}^{(i)}\leq\max\left(D_{1},M^{(i)},M_{\rm mult}^{(2)},1\right),

where
D_{4}=D_{4}(D_{3})
is a large enough constant.
Since
f
is a linear combination of
f_{\rm mult}^{(2)}(f_{\rm swit}^{(i)}(\cdot),f_{i}(\cdot))
for each
i\in[D_{1}]
, Lemma
9
, Lemma
10
and Lemma
11
implies that
f\circ\rho_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L,{\bf d},s,M)
with

\displaystyle L\leq\max_{i\in[D_{1}]}\widetilde{L}^{(i)}+3\leq D_{5}\log(1/\epsilon)\log\log(1/\epsilon)

\displaystyle\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\max\left(2\sum_{i=1}^{D_{1}}\|\widetilde{\bf d}^{(i)}\|_{\infty},4D_{1}\right)\leq D_{5}\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{3}

\displaystyle s\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{D_{1}}\left(\widetilde{s}^{(i)}+\max_{j\in[D_{1}]}\widetilde{L}^{(j)}\right)+4D_{1}+4\leq D_{5}\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{4}

\displaystyle M\leq\max_{i\in[D_{1}]}\left(\widetilde{M}^{(i)}\vee e^{-\underline{T}_{i}}\right)\leq D_{5}\epsilon^{-1}

for large enough constant
D_{5}=D_{5}(D_{4})
.
Note that
|(f\circ\rho_{1})(\widetilde{x})-e^{-x}|\leq|(f\circ\rho_{1})(\widetilde{x})-e^{-(\widetilde{x}\vee 0)}|\ +|e^{-(\widetilde{x}\vee 0)}-e^{-x}|\leq\epsilon+|x-\widetilde{x}|
for any
x\geq 0
and
\widetilde{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Then, the assertion follows by re-defining the constant.
∎

Lemma 18

(
\mu_{t}
and
\sigma_{t}
)
.

For any
0<\epsilon<1/2
, there exists a positive constant
C_{N,4}=C_{N,4}(\underline{\tau},\overline{\tau})
and neural networks
f_{\mu}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L_{\mu},{\bf d}_{\mu},s_{\mu},M_{\mu}),f_{\sigma}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L_{\sigma},{\bf d}_{\sigma},s_{\sigma},M_{\sigma})
with

\displaystyle L_{\mu},L_{\sigma}\leq C_{N,4}\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{2},\quad\|{\bf d}_{\mu}\|_{\infty},\|{\bf d}_{\sigma}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{N,4}\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{2}

\displaystyle s_{\mu},s_{\sigma}\leq C_{N,4}\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{3},\quad M_{\mu},M_{\sigma}\leq C_{N,4}\log(1/\epsilon)

such that

\displaystyle\left|\mu_{t_{1}}-f_{\mu}(t_{1})\right|\leq\epsilon\quad\quad\text{and}\quad\quad\left|\sigma_{t_{2}}-f_{\sigma}(t_{2})\right|\leq\epsilon

for any
t_{1}\geq 0
and
t_{2}\geq\epsilon
.

Proof.

This is a re-statement of Lemma B.1 in
Oko et al. (2023)
.
∎

Lemma 19

(Reciprocal function)
.

For any
0<\epsilon<1
, there exists a positive constant
C_{N,5}
and a neural network
f_{\rm rec}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L,{\bf d},s,M)
with

\displaystyle L\leq C_{N,5}\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{2},\quad\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{N,5}\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{3}

\displaystyle s\leq C_{N,5}\{\log(1/\epsilon)\}^{4},\quad M\leq C_{N,5}\epsilon^{-2}

such that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{x}-f_{\rm rec}(\widetilde{x})\right|\leq\epsilon+\frac{|x-\widetilde{x}|}{\epsilon^{2}}

for any
x\in[\epsilon,1/\epsilon]
and
\widetilde{x}\in{\mathbb{R}}
.

Proof.

This is a re-statement of Lemma F.7 in
Oko et al. (2023)
.
∎

Appendix B
Proofs for the Approximation Theory

In this section, we provide the proof of Theorem
5
.
We begin by outlining the crucial lemmas and propositions.

For
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, let
P_{n}
be the Legendre polynomial of degree
n
defined as

\displaystyle P_{n}(x)=\left(\frac{1}{2^{n}{n!}}\right)\frac{{\rm d}^{n}}{{\rm d}x^{n}}(x^{2}-1)^{n}

for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
It is well-known (page 114 of
Arnold (2004)
) that equation
P_{n}=0
has
n
distinct roots
\widetilde{x}_{1}^{(n)},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{n}^{(n)}
satisfying
-1<\widetilde{x}_{1}^{(n)}<\cdots<\widetilde{x}_{n}^{(n)}<1
.
Let
\{\widetilde{w}_{1}^{(n)},\ldots,\widetilde{w}_{n}^{(n)}\}
be the Gauss-Legendre quadrature weights, that is,

\displaystyle\widetilde{w}_{j}^{(n)}=\begin{cases}\int_{-1}^{1}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1\\
k\neq j\end{subarray}}^{n}\left(\frac{x-\widetilde{x}_{k}^{(n)}}{\widetilde{x}_{j}^{(n)}-\widetilde{x}_{k}^{(n)}}\right){\rm d}x,&\text{if }n\geq 2\\
2,&\text{if }n=1.\end{cases}

Let
n_{\beta}
be the largest integer strictly smaller than
\beta\vee 2
.
For simplicity, we denote the vectors
(\widetilde{x}_{1}^{(n_{\beta})},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{n_{\beta}}^{(n_{\beta})})
and
(\widetilde{w}_{1}^{(n_{\beta})},\ldots,\widetilde{w}_{n_{\beta}}^{(n_{\beta})})
as
(\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{n_{\beta}})
and
(\widetilde{w}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{w}_{n_{\beta}})
, respectively.
The following lemma provides an error bound for the
m
-points quadrature rule to approximate a one-dimensional integral.

Lemma 20

(
1
-dimensional
m
-point quadrature rule)
.

Let
A<B
and
\beta,K>0
be given.
For every
m\in{n_{\beta}}{\mathbb{N}}
, there exists
(w_{i},x_{i})_{i\in[m]}
with
w_{i}>0
and
x_{i}\in(A,B)
such that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{A}^{B}g(x){\rm d}x-\sum_{i=1}^{m}w_{i}g(x_{i})\right|\leq\left\{\frac{{{n_{\beta}}}^{\beta}}{2^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\lfloor\beta\rfloor!}\right\}K(B-A)^{\beta+1}m^{-\beta},

for every
g\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta,K}_{1}([A,B])
.
More specifically, one can choose

\displaystyle w_{i}=\frac{(B-A){n_{\beta}}}{2m}\widetilde{w}_{i-n_{\beta}\lfloor i/{n_{\beta}}\rfloor},

\displaystyle x_{i}=A+\frac{(B-A){n_{\beta}}}{2m}\left\{\widetilde{x}_{i-n_{\beta}\lfloor i/{n_{\beta}}\rfloor}+2\left\lfloor i/{n_{\beta}}\right\rfloor+1\right\}.

Let
\phi
be the
one
-dimensional standard normal density.
The following lemma provides a bound for the Hölder-norm of a function multiplied by
\phi
and its derivative
\phi^{\prime}
.

Lemma 21

(Preservation of Hölder continuity)
.

Let
\beta,K>0,a,b\in{\mathbb{R}}
be given and
g\in\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta,K}([a,b])
. Then, there exists a positive constant
C_{G,1}=C_{G,1}(\beta)
such that
g\phi\in\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta,KC_{G,1}}([a,b])
and
g\phi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta,KC_{G,1}}([a,b])
.

For
\mu,\sigma>0
, define
p_{\mu,\sigma}(\cdot)
as

\displaystyle p_{\mu,\sigma}({\bf x})=\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf y})\phi_{\sigma}({\bf x}-\mu{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}=\mu^{-D}\int_{\|{\bf x}+\sigma{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu}p_{0}\left(\frac{{\bf x}+\sigma{\bf y}}{\mu}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{D}\phi(y_{i}){\rm d}{\bf y}.

Since
p_{0}
is
\beta
-smooth under the (
S
) assumption, we can approximate the integral using the quadrature method with Lemma
20
and Lemma
21
.
Note however that
{\bf y}
in RHS ranges over a large set for small
\sigma
, and the error bound given in Lemma
20
depends polynomially on the size of the interval.
Since the tail of
\phi
decays very quickly, one can control the numerical error as in the following lemma.

Lemma 22

(Quadrature rule for
p_{\mu,\sigma}({\bf x})
and
\nabla p_{\mu,\sigma}({\bf x})
)
.

Let
\beta,K>0
be given and suppose that true density
p_{0}
belongs to
\mathcal{H}^{\beta,K}([-1,1]^{D})
.
For
\tau_{\rm bd},\tau_{\rm tail},\mu,\sigma>0
,
m\in n_{\beta}{\mathbb{N}}
,
i\in[D]
,
j\in[m]
and
{\bf x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{D})^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
, let

\displaystyle y_{j}^{(i)}=2\sqrt{2\tau_{\rm tail}}\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{\tau_{\rm bd}+\frac{1}{2}}\left\{-x_{i}-\mu+\frac{{n_{\beta}}\mu}{m}\left(\widetilde{x}_{j-n_{\beta}\lfloor j/n_{\beta}\rfloor}+2\left\lfloor j/n_{\beta}\right\rfloor+1\right)\right\},

\displaystyle w_{j}=2\sqrt{2\tau_{\rm tail}}{n_{\beta}}\widetilde{w}_{j-n_{\beta}\lfloor j/n_{\beta}\rfloor}\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{\tau_{\rm bd}+\frac{1}{2}}.

For
{\bf j}=(j_{1},\ldots,j_{D})^{\top}\in[m]^{D}
, let
{\widetilde{\bf y}}_{{\bf j}}=(y_{j_{1}}^{(1)},\ldots,y_{j_{D}}^{(D)})^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{{\bf x}+\sigma\widetilde{\bf y}_{{\bf j}}}{\mu}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 1-\frac{\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{-\tau_{\rm bd}}}{2},\quad\quad{\bf j}\in[m]^{D},

\displaystyle\mu^{D}\left|p_{\mu,\sigma}({\bf x})-\frac{1}{m^{D}}\sum_{{\bf j}\in[m]^{D}}\left\{\prod_{i=1}^{D}w_{j_{i}}\phi(y_{j_{i}}^{(i)})\right\}p_{0}\left(\frac{{\bf x}+\sigma\widetilde{\bf y}_{{\bf j}}}{\mu}\right)\right|\leq\epsilon\quad\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\mu^{D}\left\|\sigma\nabla p_{\mu,\sigma}({\bf x})-\frac{1}{m^{D}}\sum_{{\bf j}\in[m]^{D}}\widetilde{\bf y}_{{\bf j}}\left\{\prod_{i=1}^{D}w_{j_{i}}\phi(y_{j_{i}}^{(i)})\right\}p_{0}\left(\frac{{\bf x}+\sigma\widetilde{\bf y}_{{\bf j}}}{\mu}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\epsilon

for every
\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu-\mu\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{-\tau_{\rm bd}},\mu\in[1/2,1],\sigma\in(0,\widetilde{C}_{2}]
, where
\widetilde{C}_{1}=\widetilde{C}_{1}(\beta,D,\tau_{\rm tail}),

\widetilde{C}_{2}=\widetilde{C}_{2}(\beta,D,\tau_{\rm bd},\tau_{\rm tail})
and

\displaystyle\epsilon=\widetilde{C}_{1}K\left(\sigma^{\tau_{\rm tail}}+m^{-\beta}\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{{(\tau_{\rm bd}+\frac{1}{2})(\beta+1)}}\right)\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{(\tau_{\rm bd}+\frac{1}{2})(D-1)}.

We can approximate the maps
({\bf x},t)\mapsto p_{t}({\bf x})
and
({\bf x},t)\mapsto\nabla p_{t}({\bf x})
using deep ReLU networks by replacing
(\mu,\sigma)
in Lemma
22
with
(\mu_{t},\sigma_{t})
.
As discussed in Section
5.3
, a weight-sharing network is used to reduce the number of distinct network parameters.
The approximation result is provided in the following proposition.

Proposition 23

(Approximation at the interior of near-support)
.

Suppose the true density
p_{0}
satisfies the assumption (
S
) and

\displaystyle\tau_{\rm bd}\in 1/2+{\mathbb{N}},\quad\tau_{\rm tail}>0,\quad\tau_{\rm min}\geq\frac{4\beta}{d(\beta\wedge 1)}.

Then, for every
m\geq\widetilde{C}_{5}
, there exists a class of permutation matrices
\mathcal{P}=\{\mathcal{Q}_{i},\mathcal{R}_{i}\}_{i\in[L-1]}
and weight-sharing network
{\bf f}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm WSNN}(L,{\bf d},s,M,\mathcal{P}_{{\bf m}})

with

\displaystyle L\leq\widetilde{C}_{3}(\log m)^{2}\log\log m,\quad\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}\leq\widetilde{C}_{3}m^{D+1},

\displaystyle s\leq\widetilde{C}_{3}m(\log m)^{5}\log\log m,\quad M\leq\exp\left(\widetilde{C}_{3}\{\log m\}^{2}\right),

\displaystyle\|{\bf m}\|_{\infty}\leq\widetilde{C}_{3}m^{D}

satisfying

\displaystyle\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\sigma_{t}\nabla p_{t}({\bf x})\\
p_{t}({\bf x})\end{pmatrix}-{\bf f}({\bf x},t)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\widetilde{C}_{4}\left(\log m\right)^{(\tau_{\rm bd}+\frac{1}{2})(D-1)}\left\{t^{\frac{\tau_{\rm tail}}{2}}+m^{-\frac{\beta}{d}}\left(\log m\right)^{(\tau_{\rm bd}+\frac{1}{2})(\beta+1)}\right\}

for every
{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
with
\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu_{t}-\mu_{t}\{\log(1/\sigma_{t})\}^{-\tau_{\rm bd}}
and
m^{-\tau_{\rm min}}\leq t\leq\overline{\tau}^{-1}(\widetilde{C}_{2}^{2}\wedge 1/2)
.

Here,
\widetilde{C}_{3}=\widetilde{C}_{3}(\beta,d,D,K,\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau},\tau_{\rm bd},\tau_{\rm tail},\tau_{\rm min})
,

\widetilde{C}_{4}=\widetilde{C}_{4}(\beta,d,D,K,\underline{\tau},\tau_{\rm bd},\tau_{\rm tail},\tau_{\rm min})
,

\widetilde{C}_{5}=\widetilde{C}_{5}(\beta,d,\underline{\tau},\tau_{\rm bd},\tau_{\rm tail},\tau_{\min})

and

\widetilde{C}_{2}=\widetilde{C}_{2}(\beta,D,\tau_{\rm bd},\tau_{\rm tail})
be the constant in Lemma
22
,

As
t\to 0
,
p_{t}
is not lower bounded near the boundary of the support of
p_{0}
due to the lower bound condition, making the approximation of
\nabla\log p_{t}
challenging.
With the assumption (
B
),
p_{0}
is infinitely smooth so one can approximate
p_{0}
efficiently with local polynomials by applying Taylor’s theorem in the low-density region.
Since a Gaussian density can also be efficiently approximated with local polynomials, one can calculate the integral in
p_{t}
closed form, and approximate the output with vanilla feedforward neural networks.
The following proposition provides the approximation result, and our main proof strategy follows the proofs of Lemma B.2–Lemma B.5 from
Oko et al. (2023)
, with modifications for simplification.

Proposition 24

(Approximation at the boundary of near-support)
.

Let
K,\tau_{\rm bd},\tau_{\rm x}>0
,
0<\tau_{\rm t}<1
,
0<\widetilde{\tau}_{\rm bd}<\tau_{\rm bd}
be given and suppose the true density
p_{0}
satisfies that
\|p_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq K
.
Then, for
0<\delta\leq\widetilde{C}_{8}
and
p_{0}
satisfying

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{\alpha}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{D}}\sup_{1-\{\log(1/\delta)\}^{-\widetilde{\tau}_{\rm bd}}\leq\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\left|({\rm D}^{\bm{\alpha}}p_{0})({\bf x})\right|\leq K,

there exists a network
{\bf f}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L,{\bf d},s,M)
with

\displaystyle L\leq\widetilde{C}_{6}\{\log(1/\delta)\}^{4},\quad\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}\leq\widetilde{C}_{6}\{\log(1/\delta)\}^{7+D\widetilde{\tau}_{\rm bd}+D},

\displaystyle s\leq\widetilde{C}_{6}\{\log(1/\delta)\}^{11+D\widetilde{\tau}_{\rm bd}+D},\quad M\leq\exp\left(\widetilde{C}_{6}\{\log(1/\delta)\}^{2}\right),

satisfying

\displaystyle\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\sigma_{t}\nabla p_{t}({\bf x})\\
p_{t}({\bf x})\end{pmatrix}-{\bf f}({\bf x},t)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\widetilde{C}_{7}\delta\{\log(1/\delta)\}^{D}

for every
{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
with
\mu_{t}-\tau_{\rm x}\{\log(1/\sigma_{t})\}^{-\tau_{\rm bd}}\leq\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu_{t}+\tau_{\rm x}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}
and
\delta\leq t\leq\delta^{\tau_{\rm t}}
.

Here,
\widetilde{C}_{6}=\widetilde{C}_{6}(D,K,\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau},\tau_{\rm x})
,
\widetilde{C}_{7}=\widetilde{C}_{7}(D,K,\underline{\tau})
,
\widetilde{C}_{8}=\widetilde{C}_{8}(D,\overline{\tau},\tau_{\rm bd},\tau_{\rm x},\tau_{\rm t},\widetilde{\tau}_{\rm bd})
are positive constants.

For
t_{*}\geq 0
and
t>0
, we have

\displaystyle p_{t_{*}+t}({\bf x})=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}p_{t_{*}}({\bf y})\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y},\quad{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}

due to the Markov property of the process
({\bf X}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
.
Note that the map
{\bf x}\mapsto p_{t_{*}}({\bf x})
is infinitely differentiable and its norm is bounded as
\|{\rm D}^{{\bf k}}p_{t_{*}}(\cdot)\|_{\infty}\lesssim t_{*}^{-k./2}
for any
{\bf k}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{D}
; see Lemma
8
).
Then, one can approximate
p_{t_{*}}
with a local Taylor expansion, yielding an error
O(m^{-k./D}t_{*}^{-k./2})
using grid points bounded by
O(m)
, both up to a poly-logarithmic factor.
Similar to the proof of Proposition
24
, one can approximate the map
({\bf x},t)\mapsto p_{t_{*}+t}({\bf x})
with vanilla feedforward neural networks.
The following proposition provides the approximation result, and our main proof strategy follows the proof of Lemma B.7 from
Oko et al. (2023)
, with modifications for simplification.

Proposition 25

(Approximation for large
t
)
.

Let
K,\tau_{1},\tau_{\rm x}>0,\tau_{\rm sm}\in{\mathbb{N}},\tau_{\rm low}\in(0,1)
be given and suppose the true density
p_{0}
satisfies that
\tau_{1}\leq p_{0}({\bf x})\leq K
for any
{\bf x}\in[-1,1]^{D}
.
Then, for
m\geq\widetilde{C}_{11}
, there exists a neural network
{\bf f}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L,{\bf d},s,M)
with

\displaystyle L\leq\widetilde{C}_{9}(\log m)^{4},\quad\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}\leq\widetilde{C}_{9}m(\log m)^{9},

\displaystyle s\leq\widetilde{C}_{9}m(\log m)^{9},\quad M\leq\exp(\widetilde{C}_{9}(\log m)^{2})

such that

\displaystyle\left\|\begin{pmatrix}\sigma_{t}\nabla p_{t_{*}+t}({\bf x})\\
p_{t_{*}+t}({\bf x})\end{pmatrix}-{\bf f}({\bf x},t)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\widetilde{C}_{10}m^{-\frac{\tau_{\rm low}\tau_{\rm sm}-(D+1-\tau_{\rm low})D}{D(1+D)}}(\log m)^{D(\frac{\tau_{\rm sm}}{2}+1)}

for every
{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
with
\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu_{t}+\tau_{\rm x}\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}
,
\delta\leq t\leq\overline{\tau}^{-1}\log(1/\delta)
, where

\displaystyle t_{*}=m^{-\frac{2-2\tau_{\rm low}}{D}}\quad\text{and}\quad\delta=m^{-\frac{\tau_{\rm low}\tau_{\rm sm}+D+1-\tau_{\rm low}}{D(1+D)}}.

Here,
\widetilde{C}_{9},\widetilde{C}_{10},\widetilde{C}_{11}
are positive constants depending on
(D,K,\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau},\tau_{\rm x},\tau_{\rm sm},\tau_{\rm low})
.

B.1
Proofs of Lemma
20
to
22

In this subsection, we provide the proof of Lemma
20
, Lemma
21
, and Lemma
22
.

By the definition, we have
\exp(-\overline{\tau}t)\leq\mu_{t}\leq\exp(-\underline{\tau}t)
and
1-\exp(-2\underline{\tau}t)\leq\sigma_{t}^{2}\leq 1-\exp(-2\overline{\tau}t)
for
t\geq 0
. Since
x/2\leq 1-e^{-x}
for
0\leq x\leq 1
and
1-e^{-x}\leq x
for
x\geq 0
, we have

\begin{split}&\mu_{t}\leq 1-\frac{\underline{\tau}t}{2}\leq 1,\quad\sigma_{t}\leq\sqrt{1-\exp(-2\overline{\tau}t)}\leq\sqrt{2\overline{\tau}t},\quad\forall t\geq 0,\quad\text{and}\\
&\mu_{t}\geq 1-\overline{\tau}t\geq\frac{1}{2},\quad\sigma_{t}\geq\sqrt{1-\exp(-2\underline{\tau}t)}\geq\sqrt{\underline{\tau}t},\quad\forall 0\leq t\leq(2\overline{\tau})^{-1}.\end{split}

(27)

The last display is widely used in the following proofs.

B.1.1
Proof of Lemma
20

Proof.

Let
m_{0}=\frac{2m}{(B-A){n_{\beta}}}
and consider a function
g\in\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta,K}([A,B])
.
Simple calculation yields that

\int_{A}^{B}g(x){\rm d}x=\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{m_{0}(B-A)}{2}}\int_{A+\frac{2(i-1)}{m_{0}}}^{A+\frac{2i}{m_{0}}}g(x){\rm d}x=\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{m_{0}(B-A)}{2}}\int_{-\frac{1}{m_{0}}}^{\frac{1}{m_{0}}}g_{i}(x){\rm d}x,

(28)

where
g_{i}(x)=g(x+A+\frac{2i-1}{m_{0}})
.
For each
i\in\{1,\ldots,\frac{m_{0}(B-A)}{2}\}
, let
L_{i}
be the Lagrange interpolating polynomial of degree
{n_{\beta}}-1
that agrees with the function
g_{i}
at knots
\{\widetilde{x}_{1}/m_{0},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{{n_{\beta}}}/m_{0}\}
, defined as

\displaystyle L_{i}(x)=\sum_{j=1}^{{n_{\beta}}}g_{i}(\widetilde{x}_{j}/m_{0})l_{j}(x),

where

\displaystyle l_{j}(x)=\begin{cases}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=1\\
k\neq j\end{subarray}}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\frac{m_{0}x-\widetilde{x}_{k}}{\widetilde{x}_{j}-\widetilde{x}_{k}},&\text{if }\beta>2,\\
1,&\text{if }0<\beta\leq 2,\end{cases}

for
j\in[{n_{\beta}}]
.
Note that
L_{i}(\widetilde{x}_{j}/m_{0})=g_{i}(\widetilde{x}_{j}/m_{0})
for any
j\in[{n_{\beta}}]
.

If
0<\beta\leq 1
,
\widetilde{x}_{1}=0
and
L_{i}(x)=g_{i}(0)
. Since
g_{i}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta,K}_{1}([-1/m_{0},1/m_{0}])
, we have

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{-\frac{1}{m_{0}}}^{\frac{1}{m_{0}}}\left\{g_{i}(x)-L_{i}(x)\right\}{\rm d}x\right|\leq\int_{-\frac{1}{m_{0}}}^{\frac{1}{m_{0}}}|g_{i}(x)-L_{i}(x)|{\rm d}x\\
&\leq\int_{-\frac{1}{m_{0}}}^{\frac{1}{m_{0}}}K|x|^{\beta}{\rm d}x\leq\frac{2}{m_{0}}Km_{0}^{-\beta}=2Km_{0}^{-(\beta+1)}.\end{split}

(29)

If
\beta>1
, fix
\widetilde{x}_{0}\in[-1,1]
satisfying
\widetilde{x}_{0}\neq\widetilde{x}_{j}
for
j\in[\lfloor\beta\rfloor]
. Consider a function
h:[-1/m_{0},1/m_{0}]\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\displaystyle h(x)=g_{i}(x)-L_{i}(x)-\left\{g_{i}\left(\frac{\widetilde{x}_{0}}{m_{0}}\right)-L_{i}\left(\frac{\widetilde{x}_{0}}{m_{0}}\right)\right\}\prod_{j=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left(\frac{m_{0}x-\widetilde{x}_{j}}{\widetilde{x}_{0}-\widetilde{x}_{j}}\right).

Then,
h(\widetilde{x}_{j}/m_{0})=0
for
j\in\{0,\ldots,\lfloor\beta\rfloor\}
and
g
is
\lfloor\beta\rfloor
-times differentiable on
(-1/m_{0},1/m_{0})
. Generalized Rolle’s Theorem (see Theorem 1.10 of
Burden and Faires (2010)
) implies that there exists a constant
\xi_{\widetilde{x}_{0}}\in(-1/m_{0},1/m_{0})
such that
({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}h)(\xi_{\widetilde{x}_{0}})=0
. Since
L_{i}
is the polynomial of degree less than
\lfloor\beta\rfloor
and
({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}L_{i})(\xi_{\widetilde{x}_{0}})=0
, a simple calculation yields that

\displaystyle g_{i}\left(\frac{\widetilde{x}_{0}}{m_{0}}\right)=L_{i}\left(\frac{\widetilde{x}_{0}}{m_{0}}\right)+\frac{({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g_{i})(\xi_{\widetilde{x}_{0}})}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor!}\prod_{j=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left(\frac{\widetilde{x}_{0}-\widetilde{x}_{j}}{m_{0}}\right).

Note that
g_{i}(\widetilde{x}_{j}/m_{0})=L_{i}(\widetilde{x}_{j}/m_{0})
for
j\in[\lfloor\beta\rfloor]
. Combining with the last display, there exists a function
\xi:[-1/m_{0},1/m_{0}]\to(-1/m_{0},1/m_{0})
such that

\displaystyle g_{i}(x)=L_{i}(x)+\frac{({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g_{i})(\xi(x))}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor!}\prod_{j=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left(x-\frac{\widetilde{x}_{j}}{m_{0}}\right),\quad x\in\left[-\frac{1}{m_{0}},\frac{1}{m_{0}}\right],

where
\xi(x)=0
for
x\in\{\frac{\widetilde{x}_{1}}{m_{0}},\ldots,\frac{\widetilde{x}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}}{m_{0}}\}
. For
x\in[-1/m_{0},1/m_{0}]
, we have

\displaystyle\left|g_{i}(x)-L_{i}(x)-\frac{({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g_{i})(0)}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor!}\prod_{j=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left(x-\frac{\widetilde{x}_{j}}{m_{0}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left\{\frac{({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g_{i})(\xi(x))-({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g_{i})(0)}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor!}\right\}\prod_{j=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left(x-\frac{\widetilde{x}_{j}}{m_{0}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K|\xi(x)|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor!}\prod_{j=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left(\frac{2}{m_{0}}\right)\leq\frac{K2^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor!}m_{0}^{-\beta},

where the first inequality holds because
g_{i}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta,K}_{1}([-1/m_{0},1/m_{0}])
. Since
\{\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\}
are the roots of the Legendre polynomial, its orthogonality implies that
\int_{-1/m_{0}}^{1/m_{0}}\prod_{j=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}(x-\frac{\widetilde{x}_{j}}{m_{0}}){\rm d}x=0
. Combining with the last display, it follows that

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{-\frac{1}{m_{0}}}^{\frac{1}{m_{0}}}\left\{g_{i}(x)-L_{i}(x)\right\}{\rm d}x\right|\\
&=\left|\int_{-\frac{1}{m_{0}}}^{\frac{1}{m_{0}}}\left\{g_{i}(x)-L_{i}(x)-\frac{({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g_{i})(0)}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor!}\prod_{j=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left(x-\frac{\widetilde{x}_{j}}{m_{0}}\right)\right\}{\rm d}x\right|\\
&\leq\int_{-\frac{1}{m_{0}}}^{\frac{1}{m_{0}}}\left|g_{i}(x)-L_{i}(x)-\frac{({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g_{i})(0)}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor!}\prod_{j=1}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left(x-\frac{\widetilde{x}_{j}}{m_{0}}\right)\right|{\rm d}x\leq\frac{K2^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor!}m_{0}^{-(\beta+1)}.\end{split}

(30)

A simple calculation yields that

\displaystyle\int_{-\frac{1}{m_{0}}}^{\frac{1}{m_{0}}}L_{i}(x)=\sum_{j=1}^{{n_{\beta}}}g_{i}\left(\frac{\widetilde{x}_{j}}{m_{0}}\right)\left\{\int_{-\frac{1}{m_{0}}}^{\frac{1}{m_{0}}}l_{j}(x){\rm d}x\right\}=\sum_{j=1}^{{n_{\beta}}}\frac{\widetilde{w}_{j}}{m_{0}}g_{i}\left(\frac{\widetilde{x}_{j}}{m_{0}}\right).

Combining (
28
), (
29
) and (
30
) with the last display, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{A}^{B}g(x){\rm d}x-\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{m_{0}(B-A)}{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{{n_{\beta}}}\frac{\widetilde{w}_{j}}{m_{0}}g_{i}\left(\frac{\widetilde{x}_{j}}{m_{0}}\right)\right|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{m_{0}(B-A)}{2}}\left|\int_{-\frac{1}{m_{0}}}^{\frac{1}{m_{0}}}\left\{g_{i}(x)-L_{i}(x)\right\}{\rm d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K(B-A)2^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor!}m_{0}^{-\beta}=\frac{{(\lfloor\beta\rfloor\vee 1)}^{\beta}}{2^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\lfloor\beta\rfloor!}(B-A)^{\beta+1}m^{-\beta}.

Then, the assertion follows because
\sum_{i=1}^{m}w_{i}g(x_{i})=\sum_{i=1}^{m_{0}(B-A)/2}\sum_{j=1}^{{n_{\beta}}}\frac{\widetilde{w}_{j}}{m_{0}}g_{i}\left(\frac{\widetilde{x}_{j}}{m_{0}}\right)
.
∎

B.1.2
Proof of Lemma
21

Proof.

For any
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
, it is well-known (see
(
Indritz, 1961
)
) that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{{\rm d}^{n}}{{\rm d}x^{n}}e^{-x^{2}}\right|\leq\sqrt{2^{n}n!}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2}},\quad x\in{\mathbb{R}}

and moreover,

\|{\rm D}^{n}\phi\|_{\infty}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left\|\frac{{\rm d}^{n}}{{\rm d}x^{n}}e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{2}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\sqrt{n!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left\|e^{-\frac{x^{2}}{4}}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\sqrt{n!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}},

(31)

where the first inequality holds by the chain rule.
Then,

\begin{split}&\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha}(g\phi)\right\|_{\infty}=\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left\|\sum_{r=0}^{\alpha}\binom{\alpha}{r}({\rm D}^{r}g)({\rm D}^{\alpha-r}\phi)\right\|_{\infty}\\
&\leq\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\sum_{r=0}^{\alpha}\binom{\alpha}{r}\left\|{\rm D}^{r}g\right\|_{\infty}\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha-r}\phi\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\sum_{r=0}^{\alpha}\alpha^{\alpha}\left\|{\rm D}^{r}g\right\|_{\infty}\frac{\sqrt{(\alpha-r)!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\\
&\leq\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}K\alpha^{\alpha}\frac{\sqrt{\alpha!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\leq K\left(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1\right)^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\frac{\sqrt{\lfloor\beta\rfloor!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}.\end{split}

(32)

Similarly, we have

\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha}(g\phi^{\prime})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\sum_{r=0}^{\alpha}\binom{\alpha}{r}\left\|{\rm D}^{r}g\right\|_{\infty}\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha-r+1}\phi\right\|_{\infty}\leq K\left(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1\right)^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\frac{\sqrt{({\lfloor\beta\rfloor}+1)!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}.

(33)

Let
A=[a,b]
.
For any differentiable function
h:A\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
and
0<\gamma\leq 1
, we have

\begin{split}&\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
x\neq y\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|h(x)-h(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\gamma}}\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
x\neq y\\
|x-y|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|h(x)-h(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\gamma}}+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
|x-y|\geq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|h(x)-h(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\gamma}}\\
&\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
x\neq y\\
|x-y|\leq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|h(x)-h(y)\right|}{|x-y|}+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
|x-y|\geq 1\end{subarray}}{\left|h(x)-h(y)\right|}\leq\left\|h^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}+2\left\|h\right\|_{\infty}.\end{split}

(34)

If
\beta>1
, it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{\left|({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}(g\phi))(x)-({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}(g\phi))(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left|\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\binom{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}{\alpha}\left\{({\rm D}^{\alpha}g)(x)({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha}\phi)(x)-({\rm D}^{\alpha}g)(y)({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha}\phi)(y)\right\}\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\binom{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}{\alpha}\left|({\rm D}^{\alpha}g)(x)\right|\left(\frac{\left|({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha}\phi)(x)-({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha}\phi)(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\binom{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}{\alpha}\left|({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha}\phi)(y)\right|\left(\frac{\left|({\rm D}^{\alpha}g)(x)-(D^{\alpha}g)(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\binom{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}{\alpha}\left\|D^{\alpha}g\right\|_{\infty}\left(\left\|{\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha+1}\phi\right\|_{\infty}+2\left\|{\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha}\phi\right\|_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-1}\binom{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}{\alpha}\left\|{\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha}\phi\right\|_{\infty}\left(\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha+1}g\right\|_{\infty}+2\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha}g\right\|_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\|\phi\|_{\infty}\left(\frac{\left|({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g)(x)-({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g)(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}}\right)

for any
x,y\in A
with
x\neq y
, where the last inequality holds by (
34
).
Combining (
31
) with the last display, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
x\neq y\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}(g\phi))(x)-({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}(g\phi))(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\binom{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}{\alpha}\left\|D^{\alpha}g\right\|_{\infty}\left(\left\|{\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha+1}\phi\right\|_{\infty}+2\left\|{\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha}\phi\right\|_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-1}\binom{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}{\alpha}\left\|{\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha}\phi\right\|_{\infty}\left(\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha+1}g\right\|_{\infty}+2\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha}g\right\|_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\|\phi\|_{\infty}\left(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
x\neq y\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g)(x)-(D^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g)(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}^{\alpha}\left\|D^{\alpha}g\right\|_{\infty}\left(\frac{\sqrt{(\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha+1)!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}+\frac{2\sqrt{(\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha)!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-1}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}^{\alpha}\left(\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha+1}g\right\|_{\infty}+2\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha}g\right\|_{\infty}\right)\left(\frac{\sqrt{(\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha)!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
x\neq y\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g)(x)-({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g)(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}}\right).

Moreover, the last display is bounded by

\begin{split}&\leq 3\lfloor\beta\rfloor^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left(\frac{\sqrt{(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1)!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)\left(\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha}g\right\|_{\infty}\right)\\
&\quad+3\lfloor\beta\rfloor^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-1}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lfloor\beta\rfloor!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)\left(\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha}g\right\|_{\infty}+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
x\neq y\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g)(x)-({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g)(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}}\right)\\
&\leq 6K\lfloor\beta\rfloor^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left(\frac{\sqrt{(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1)!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right).\end{split}

(35)

Similarly, we have

\begin{split}&\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
x\neq y\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}(g\phi^{\prime}))(x)-({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}(g\phi^{\prime}))(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}}\\
&\leq\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\binom{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}{\alpha}\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha}g\right\|_{\infty}\left(\left\|{\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha+2}\phi\right\|_{\infty}+2\left\|{\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha+1}\phi\right\|_{\infty}\right)\\
&\quad+\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-1}\binom{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}{\alpha}\left\|{\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha+1}\phi\right\|_{\infty}\left(\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha+1}g\right\|_{\infty}+2\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha}g\right\|_{\infty}\right)\\
&\quad+\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\left(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
x\neq y\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g)(x)-({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g)(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}}\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}^{\alpha}\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha}g\right\|_{\infty}\left(\frac{\sqrt{(\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha+2)!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}+\frac{2\sqrt{(\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha+1)!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)\\
&\quad+\sum_{\alpha=0}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor-1}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}^{\alpha}\left(\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha+1}g\right\|_{\infty}+2\left\|{\rm D}^{\alpha}g\right\|_{\infty}\right)\left(\frac{\sqrt{(\lfloor\beta\rfloor-\alpha+1)!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\left(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
x\neq y\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g)(x)-({\rm D}^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}g)(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta-\lfloor\beta\rfloor}}\right)\\
&\leq 6K\lfloor\beta\rfloor^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left(\frac{\sqrt{(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2)!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right).\end{split}

(36)

If
0<\beta\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
x\neq y\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|(g\phi)(x)-(g\phi)(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta}}\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
x\neq y\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|g(x)\phi(x)-g(x)\phi(y)+g(x)\phi(y)-g(y)\phi(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq\|g\|_{\infty}\left(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
x\neq y\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|\phi(x)-\phi(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta}}\right)+\|\phi\|_{\infty}\left(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
x\neq y\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|g(x)-g(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\|g\|_{\infty}+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
x\neq y\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|g(x)-g(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta}}\right)\left(\left\|\phi^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}+2\left\|\phi\right\|_{\infty}\right)\leq\frac{3K}{\sqrt{2\pi}}

and

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
x\neq y\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|(g\phi^{\prime})(x)-(g\phi^{\prime})(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta}}\leq\left(\|g\|_{\infty}+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x,y\in A\\
x\neq y\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|g(x)-g(y)\right|}{|x-y|^{\beta}}\right)\left(\left\|\phi^{\prime\prime}\right\|_{\infty}+2\left\|\phi^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\sqrt{2}K}{\sqrt{2\pi}},

where the second and third inequality holds by (
34
) and (
31
), respectively.
Combining (
32
), (
33
), (
35
), (
36
) with the last display, we have
g\phi\in\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta,KD_{1}}(A)
and
g\phi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta,KD_{1}}
, where

\displaystyle D_{1}=\left(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1\right)^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}\frac{\sqrt{(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1)!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}+6\lfloor\beta\rfloor^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor}\left(\frac{\sqrt{(\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2)!}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right).

The assertion follows by re-defining the constant.
∎

B.1.3
Proof of Lemma
22

Proof.

Consider
\tau_{\rm bd},\tau_{\rm tail}>0,m\in n_{\beta}{\mathbb{N}}
and
real-valued
D
-dimensional vectors
{\bf x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{D})^{\top},{\bf y}=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{D})^{\top}
such that
\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu-\mu\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{-\tau_{\rm bd}}
and
\|{\bf x}+\sigma{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu
.
Let
{\bf y}_{<i+1}=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{i})^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{i}
and
{\bf y}_{>D-i}=(y_{D-i+1},\ldots,y_{D})^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{i}
for
i\in[D]
.
For
y\in{\mathbb{R}}
and each
i
, denote
({\bf y}_{<i},y,{\bf y}_{>i})
as a
D
-dimensional vector that is identical to
{\bf y}
except for the
i
-th component, which is replaced by
y
.

Let
D_{\sigma}=2\sqrt{2\tau_{\rm tail}}\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{(\tau_{\rm bd}+\frac{1}{2})}
and fix
i\in[D]
. Then,

\displaystyle-x_{i}-\mu\leq-\mu\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{-\tau_{\rm bd}}<0\text{\quad and \quad}-x_{i}+\mu\geq\mu\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{-\tau_{\rm bd}}>0.

Moreover,
(-x_{i}-\mu)/\sigma<(-x_{i}-\mu)D_{\sigma}<0
and
(-x_{i}+\mu)/\sigma>(-x_{i}+\mu)D_{\sigma}>0
for small enough
\sigma
so that
0<D_{\sigma}\leq(2\sigma)^{-1}
.
Consider a one-dimensional real-valued function
g_{i}
such that

\displaystyle g_{i}\left(y;{\bf y}_{<i},{\bf y}_{>i}\right)=p_{0}\left(\frac{{\bf x}+\sigma({\bf y}_{<i},y,{\bf y}_{>i})}{\mu}\right)\phi(y),\quad y\in\left[(-x_{i}-\mu)/\sigma,(-x_{i}+\mu)/\sigma\right].

Then,

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{\frac{-x_{i}-\mu}{\sigma}}^{\frac{-x_{i}+\mu}{\sigma}}g_{i}\left(y;{\bf y}_{<i},{\bf y}_{>i}\right){\rm d}y-\int_{(-x_{i}-\mu)D_{\sigma}}^{(-x_{i}+\mu)D_{\sigma}}g_{i}\left(y;{\bf y}_{<i},{\bf y}_{>i}\right){\rm d}y\right|\\
&=\int_{\frac{-x_{i}-\mu}{\sigma}}^{(-x_{i}-\mu)D_{\sigma}}g_{i}\left(y;{\bf y}_{<i},{\bf y}_{>i}\right){\rm d}y+\int_{(-x_{i}+\mu)D_{\sigma}}^{\frac{-x_{i}+\mu}{\sigma}}g_{i}\left(y;{\bf y}_{<i},{\bf y}_{>i}\right){\rm d}y\\
&\leq K\int_{-\infty}^{(-x_{i}-\mu)D_{\sigma}}\phi(y){\rm d}y+K\int_{(-x_{i}+\mu)D_{\sigma}}^{\infty}\phi(y){\rm d}y\\
&\leq K\exp\left(-\frac{(-x_{i}-\mu)^{2}D_{\sigma}^{2}}{2}\right)+K\exp\left(-\frac{(-x_{i}+\mu)^{2}D_{\sigma}^{2}}{2}\right)\\
&\leq 2K\exp\left(-\tau_{\rm tail}\log(1/\sigma)\right)=2K\sigma^{\tau_{\rm tail}},\end{split}

(37)

where the second inequality holds by the tail probability of the standard normal distribution.
Let
C_{G,1}=C_{G,1}(\beta)
be the constant in Lemma
21
. Since
\sigma/\mu<1
for
\sigma<2
, Lemma
21
implies that
g_{i}\in\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\beta,KC_{G,1}}([(-x_{i}-\mu)D_{\sigma},(-x_{i}+\mu)D_{\sigma}])
.
Moreover, Lemma
20
implies that

\begin{split}&\left|\int_{(-x_{i}-\mu)D_{\sigma}}^{(-x_{i}+\mu)D_{\sigma}}g_{i}\left(y;{\bf y}_{<i},{\bf y}_{>i}\right){\rm d}y-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\widetilde{v}_{j}g_{i}\left(\widetilde{y}_{j}^{(i)};{\bf y}_{<i},{\bf y}_{>i}\right)\right|\\
&\leq\left\{\frac{2^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}{{n_{\beta}}}^{\beta}}{\lfloor\beta\rfloor!}\right\}C_{G,1}K\mu^{\beta+1}D_{\sigma}^{\beta+1}m^{-\beta},\end{split}

(38)

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{y}_{j}^{(i)}=(-x_{i}-\mu)D_{\sigma}+\frac{\mu D_{\sigma}{n_{\beta}}}{m}\left\{\widetilde{x}_{j-n_{\beta}\lfloor\frac{j}{{n_{\beta}}}\rfloor}+2\left\lfloor\frac{j}{{n_{\beta}}}\right\rfloor+1\right\},

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}_{j}=\frac{\mu D_{\sigma}{n_{\beta}}}{m}\widetilde{w}_{j-n_{\beta}\lfloor\frac{j}{{n_{\beta}}}\rfloor},

and
(-x_{i}-\mu)D_{\sigma}<\widetilde{y}_{j}^{(i)}<(-x_{i}+\mu)D_{\sigma}
for
j\in[m]
.
Combining (
37
) and (
38
), we have

\left|\int_{\frac{-x_{i}-\mu}{\sigma}}^{\frac{-x_{i}+\mu}{\sigma}}p_{0}\left(\frac{{\bf x}+\sigma({\bf y}_{<i},y,{\bf y}_{>i})}{\mu}\right)\phi(y){\rm d}y-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\widetilde{v}_{j}g_{i}\left(\widetilde{y}_{j}^{(i)};{\bf y}_{<i},{\bf y}_{>i}\right)\right|\leq\epsilon,

(39)

where
\epsilon=2K\sigma^{\tau_{\rm tail}}+2^{\lfloor\beta\rfloor+1}{{n_{\beta}}}^{\beta}C_{G,1}K\mu^{\beta+1}D_{\sigma}^{\beta+1}m^{-\beta}/\lfloor\beta\rfloor!
.
Since
|x_{i}|\leq\mu-\mu\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{-\tau_{\rm bd}}
and
\sigma D_{\sigma}\leq 1/2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{x_{i}}{2\mu}-\frac{1}{2}\leq\frac{x_{i}+\sigma D_{\sigma}(-x_{i}-\mu)}{\mu}\leq\frac{x_{i}+\sigma\widetilde{y}_{j}^{(i)}}{\mu}\leq\frac{x_{i}+\sigma D_{\sigma}(-x_{i}+\mu)}{\mu}\leq\frac{x_{i}}{2\mu}+\frac{1}{2}

and

\displaystyle\left|\frac{x_{i}+\sigma\widetilde{y}_{j}^{(i)}}{\mu}\right|\leq 1-\frac{\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{-\tau_{\rm bd}}}{2}<1.

Consider
F_{(j_{1})},\ldots,F_{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{D})}
for
j_{1},\ldots,j_{D}\in[m]
, defined as

\displaystyle F_{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{k-1})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\|{\bf x}_{>k}+\sigma{\bf y}_{>k}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu}\left\{\int_{\frac{-x_{k}-\mu}{\sigma}}^{\frac{-x_{k}+\mu}{\sigma}}p_{0}\left(\frac{{\bf x}+\sigma\left(\widetilde{y}_{j_{1}}^{(1)},\ldots,\widetilde{y}_{j_{k-1}}^{(k-1)},y,{\bf y}_{>k}^{\top}\right)^{\top}}{\mu}\right)\phi(y){\rm d}y\right\}\prod_{i=k+1}^{D}\phi(y_{i}){\rm d}{\bf y}_{>k},

for
k\in\{2,\ldots,D-1\}
,

\displaystyle F_{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{D-1})}=\int_{\frac{-x_{D}-\mu}{\sigma}}^{\frac{-x_{D}+\mu}{\sigma}}p_{0}\left(\frac{{\bf x}+\sigma\left(\widetilde{y}_{j_{1}}^{(1)},\ldots,\widetilde{y}_{j_{D-1}}^{(D-1)},y\right)^{\top}}{\mu}\right)\phi(y){\rm d}y,\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle F_{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{D})}=p_{0}\left(\frac{{\bf x}+\sigma\left(\widetilde{y}_{j_{1}}^{(1)},\ldots,\widetilde{y}_{j_{D}}^{(D)}\right)^{\top}}{\mu}\right).

For any
k\in\{2,\ldots,D-1\}
and
j_{1},\ldots,j_{D}\in[m]
, we have

\begin{split}&\left|F_{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{k-1})}-\sum_{j_{k}=1}^{m}\widetilde{v}_{j_{k}}\phi\left(\widetilde{y}_{j_{k}}^{(k)}\right)F_{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{k})}\right|\\
&\leq\epsilon\int_{\|{\bf x}_{>k}+\sigma{\bf y}_{>k}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu}\left\{\prod_{i=k+1}^{D}\phi(y_{i})\right\}{\rm d}{\bf y}_{>k}\leq\epsilon\end{split}

(40)

and

\displaystyle\left|F_{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{D-1})}-\sum_{j_{D}=1}^{m}\widetilde{v}_{j_{D}}\phi\left(\widetilde{y}_{j_{D}}^{(D)}\right)F_{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{D})}\right|\leq\epsilon,

where the first and last inequality holds by (
39
).
Note that

\displaystyle\mu^{D}p_{\mu,\sigma}({\bf x})=\int_{\|{\bf x}_{>1}+\sigma{\bf y}_{>1}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu}\left[\int_{\frac{-x_{1}-\mu}{\sigma}}^{\frac{-x_{1}+\mu}{\sigma}}\left\{p_{0}\left(\frac{{\bf x}+\sigma(y,{\bf y}_{>1}^{\top})^{\top}}{\mu}\right)\phi(y)\right\}{\rm d}y\right]\prod_{i=2}^{D}\phi(y_{i}){\rm d}{\bf y}_{>1}.

Then, we also have

\displaystyle\left|\mu^{D}p_{\mu,\sigma}({\bf x})-\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{m}\widetilde{v}_{j_{1}}\phi\left(\widetilde{y}_{j_{1}}^{(1)}\right)F_{(j_{1})}\right|\leq\epsilon\int_{\|{\bf x}_{>1}+\sigma{\bf y}_{>1}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu}\left\{\prod_{i=2}^{D}\phi(y_{i})\right\}{\rm d}{\bf y}_{>1}\leq\epsilon,

where the first inequality holds by (
39
).
Combining (
40
) with the last display, we have

\begin{split}&\left|\mu^{D}p_{\mu,\sigma}({\bf x})-\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{D}=1}^{m}\prod_{k=1}^{D}\left\{\widetilde{v}_{j_{k}}\phi\left(\widetilde{y}_{j_{k}}^{(k)}\right)\right\}F_{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{D})}\right|\\
&\leq\left|\mu^{D}p_{\mu,\sigma}({\bf x})-\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{m}\widetilde{v}_{j_{1}}\phi\left(\widetilde{y}_{j_{1}}^{(1)}\right)F_{(j_{1})}\right|\\
&\quad+\sum_{i=2}^{D}\left|\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{i-1}=1}^{m}\prod_{k=1}^{i-1}\left\{\widetilde{v}_{j_{k}}\phi\left(\widetilde{y}_{j_{k}}^{(k)}\right)\right\}\left\{F_{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{i-1})}-\sum_{j_{i}=1}^{m}\widetilde{v}_{j_{i}}\phi\left(\widetilde{y}_{j_{i}}^{(i)}\right)F_{(j_{1},\ldots,j_{i})}\right\}\right|\\
&\leq\epsilon\left(1+\sum_{i=2}^{D}\sum_{j_{1},\ldots j_{i-1}=1}^{m}\left|\prod_{k=1}^{i-1}\left\{\widetilde{v}_{j_{k}}\phi\left(\widetilde{y}_{j_{k}}^{(k)}\right)\right\}\right|\right)\\
&\leq\epsilon\left(1+\sum_{i=2}^{D}\sum_{j_{1},\ldots j_{i-1}=1}^{m}\frac{\left|\prod_{k=1}^{i-1}\widetilde{v}_{j_{k}}\right|}{(2\pi)^{\frac{i-1}{2}}}\right),\end{split}

(41)

where the last inequality holds because
|\phi|\leq 1/\sqrt{2\pi}
.
For each
j\in[m]
, we have

\displaystyle|\widetilde{v}_{j}|\leq\left\{\frac{\mu D_{\sigma}{n_{\beta}}}{m}\right\}\max\left(|\widetilde{w}_{1}|,\ldots,|\widetilde{w}_{{n_{\beta}}}|\right)=\frac{D_{1}\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{(\tau_{\rm bd}+\frac{1}{2})}}{m},

where the last inequality holds because
\mu\leq 1
and
D_{1}=2\sqrt{2\tau_{\rm tail}}{n_{\beta}}\max(|\widetilde{w}_{1}|,\ldots,|\widetilde{w}_{{n_{\beta}}}|)
.
Then,

\displaystyle 1+\sum_{i=2}^{D}\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{i-1}=1}^{m}\frac{|\prod_{k=1}^{i-1}\widetilde{v}_{j_{k}}|}{(2\pi)^{\frac{i-1}{2}}}\leq 1+\sum_{i=2}^{D}\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{i-1}=1}^{m}\left(\frac{D_{1}\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{(\tau_{\rm bd}+\frac{1}{2})}}{m\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)^{i-1}

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{i=2}^{D}\left(\frac{D_{1}\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{(\tau_{\rm bd}+\frac{1}{2})}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)^{i-1}\leq D\left(\frac{D_{1}\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{(\tau_{\rm bd}+\frac{1}{2})}}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\right)^{D-1},

where the last inequality holds for small enough
\sigma
so that
D_{1}\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{(\tau_{\rm bd}+\frac{1}{2})}\geq\sqrt{2\pi}
.
Also, there exists a constant
D_{2}=D_{2}(\beta,\tau_{\rm tail},C_{G,1})
such that

\displaystyle\epsilon\leq D_{2}K\left(\sigma^{\tau_{\rm tail}}+m^{-\beta}\{\log(1/\sigma)\}^{(\tau_{\rm bd}+\frac{1}{2})(\beta+1)}\right).

Hence,
