(96)

B.5.4
Combining into a Single Function

For large enough
m
so that
3m^{-1/(2D)}\leq(2\overline{\tau})^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\{\log(1/\sqrt{\underline{\tau}})+\tau_{\rm min}\log m/2\right\}^{-3/2}\leq\{\log(1/\sigma_{t})\}^{-3/2}\leq\left\{\log(1/\sqrt{6\overline{\tau}})+\log m/(4D)\right\}^{-3/2}

for
m^{-\tau_{\rm min}}\leq t\leq 3m^{-1/(2D)}
because
\sqrt{\underline{\tau}t}\leq\sigma_{t}\leq\sqrt{2\overline{\tau}t}
for
0\leq t\leq(2\overline{\tau})^{-1}
.
For large enough
m
so that
\log(1/\sqrt{\underline{\tau}})\leq\tau_{\rm min}\log m/2
, it follows that

\displaystyle\tau_{\rm min}^{-3/2}(\log m)^{-3/2}\leq\{\log(1/\sigma_{t})\}^{-3/2}\leq(4D)^{3/2}(\log m)^{-3/2}

for
m^{-\tau_{\rm min}}\leq t\leq 3m^{-1/(2D)}
.
Then,

\mu_{t}-\tau_{\rm min}^{3/2}\{(4D)^{3/2}+3\}\{\log(1/\sigma_{t})\}^{-3/2}<\mu_{t}-\overline{x}<\mu_{t}-\underline{x}<\mu_{t}-\{\log(1/\sigma_{t})\}^{-3/2}

(97)

for
m^{-\tau_{\rm min}}\leq t\leq 3m^{-1/(2D)}
, where

\displaystyle\overline{x}=\{(4D)^{3/2}+2\}(\log m)^{-3/2}\text{\quad and \quad}\underline{x}=\{(4D)^{3/2}+1\}(\log m)^{-3/2}.

Consider piecewise linear functions
f_{\rm swit,x}^{(1)},f_{\rm swit,x}^{(2)}:{\mathbb{R}}\to[0,1]
such that

\displaystyle f_{\rm swit,x}^{(1)}(x)=(\log m)^{3/2}\rho\left(-f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{x},\overline{x})}(x)+\overline{x}\right)=\frac{1}{\overline{x}-\underline{x}}\max\left(-(x\vee\underline{x})\wedge\overline{x}+\overline{x},0\right),

\displaystyle f_{\rm swit,x}^{(2)}(x)=(\log m)^{3/2}\rho\left(f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{x},\overline{x})}(x)-\underline{x}\right)=\frac{1}{\overline{x}-\underline{x}}\max\left((x\vee\underline{x})\wedge\overline{x}-\underline{x},0\right),

for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
, where
f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{x},\overline{x})}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(2,(1,2,1)^{\top},7,\overline{x}\vee(\log m)^{3/2})
is the neural network in Lemma
15
.
Note that
f_{\rm swit,x}^{(1)}(x)+f_{\rm swit,x}^{(2)}(x)=1
for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
, and
f_{\rm swit,x}^{(1)}(x)=0
for
x\geq\overline{x}
and
f_{\rm swit,x}^{(2)}(x)=0
for
x\leq\underline{x}
.
Combining with (
96
) and (
97
), we have

\displaystyle\left|f_{\rm swit,x}^{(1)}\left(\mu_{t}-f_{\max}({\bf x})\right)\widetilde{f}_{i}^{(1)}({\bf x},t)+f_{\rm swit,x}^{(2)}\left(\mu_{t}-f_{\max}({\bf x})\right)\widetilde{f}_{i}^{(2)}({\bf x},t)-(\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x}))_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{4}/\sigma_{t},\quad i\in[D]

for
\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}
and
m^{-\tau_{\rm min}}\leq t\leq 3m^{-\frac{1}{2D}}
, where
f_{\rm max}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is a function such that

\displaystyle f_{\rm max}({\bf x})=\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\rho\left(\cdots\rho\left(\rho\left(\rho(|x_{1}|-|x_{2}|)+|x_{2}|-|x_{3}|\right)+|x_{3}|-|x_{4}|\right)\cdots+|x_{D-1}|-|x_{D}|\right)+|x_{D}|.

Note also that
|x|=\rho(x)+\rho(-x)
.
Lemma
18
implies that
there exist neural networks
f_{\mu}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(L_{\mu},{\bf d}_{\mu},s_{\mu},M_{\mu})
with

\displaystyle L_{\mu}\leq C_{N,4}\{\log(1/\delta)\}^{2},\quad,\|{\bf d}_{\mu}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{N,4}\{\log(1/\delta)\}^{2}

\displaystyle s_{\mu}\leq C_{N,4}\{\log(1/\delta)\}^{3},\quad M_{\mu}\leq C_{N,4}\log(1/\delta)

such that
|\mu_{t}-f_{\mu}(t)|\leq\delta

for
t\geq 0
.
Since
f_{\rm swit,x}^{(1)}
and
f_{\rm swit,x}^{(2)}
are
(\log m)^{\tau_{\rm bd}}
-Lipschitz continuous,
|f_{\rm swit,x}^{(i)}(\mu_{t}-\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty})-f_{\rm swit,x}^{(i)}(f_{\mu}(t)-\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty})|\leq\delta(\log m)^{\tau_{\rm bd}}
for each
i\in\{1,2\}
,
{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
and
t\geq 0
.
For
i\in[D]
, consider a function
f_{i}^{({\rm x})}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\displaystyle f_{i}^{({\rm x})}({\bf x},t)

\displaystyle=f_{\rm mult}\left(f_{\rm swit,x}^{(1)}\left(f_{\mu}(t)-f_{\rm max}({\bf x})\right),\widetilde{f}_{i}^{(1)}({\bf x},t)\right)+f_{\rm mult}\left(f_{\rm swit,x}^{(2)}\left(f_{\mu}(t)-f_{\rm max}({\bf x})\right),\widetilde{f}_{i}^{(2)}({\bf x},t)\right)

for
{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
and
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Combining with (
91
), we have

\begin{split}&\left|f_{i}^{({\rm x})}({\bf x},t)-(\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x}))_{i}\right|\\
&\leq\epsilon_{4}/\sigma_{t}+2\delta+4m^{3DD_{1}^{2}}\delta(\log m)^{\tau_{\rm bd}}\leq\epsilon_{4}/\sigma_{t}+6m^{3DD_{1}^{2}}\delta(\log m)^{\tau_{\rm bd}},\quad i\in[D]\end{split}

(98)

for
\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}
and
m^{-\tau_{\rm min}}\leq t\leq 3m^{-\frac{1}{2D}}
.
Similarly, let
\underline{t}=2m^{-\frac{1}{2D}}
and
\overline{t}=3m^{-\frac{1}{2D}}
, consider piecewise linear functions
f_{\rm swit,t}^{(1)},f_{\rm swit,t}^{(2)}:{\mathbb{R}}\to[0,1]
such that

\displaystyle f_{\rm swit,t}^{(1)}(t)=m^{\frac{1}{2D}}\rho\left(-f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{t},\overline{t})}(t)+\overline{t}\right)=\frac{1}{\overline{t}-\underline{t}}\max\left(-(t\vee\underline{t})\wedge\overline{t}+\overline{t},0\right),

\displaystyle f_{\rm swit,t}^{(2)}(t)=m^{\frac{1}{2D}}\rho\left(f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{t},\overline{t})}(t)-\underline{t}\right)=\frac{1}{\overline{t}-\underline{t}}\max\left((t\vee\underline{t})\wedge\overline{t}-\underline{t},0\right),

where
f_{\rm clip}^{(\underline{t},\overline{t})}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm NN}(2,(1,2,1)^{\top},7,\overline{t}\vee m^{\frac{1}{2D}})
is the neural network in Lemma
15
.
Note that
f_{\rm swit,t}^{(1)}(t)+f_{\rm swit,t}^{(2)}(t)=1
for
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
, and
f_{\rm swit,t}^{(1)}(t)=0
for

and
f_{\rm swit,t}^{(2)}(t)=0
for

.
Combining with (
96
) and (

), we have

\displaystyle\left|f_{\rm swit,t}^{(1)}(t)f_{i}^{({\rm x})}({\bf x},t)+f_{\rm swit,t}^{(2)}\left(t\right)\widetilde{f}_{i}^{(3)}({\bf x},t)-(\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x}))_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{4}/\sigma_{t}+6m^{3DD_{1}^{2}}\delta(\log m)^{\tau_{\rm bd}},\quad i\in[D]

for
\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}
and
m^{-\tau_{\rm min}}\leq t\leq\tau_{\rm max}\log m
.
Consider a vector-valued function
{\bf f}^{(\rm x,t)}=(f_{1}^{(\rm x,t)},\ldots,f_{D}^{(\rm x,t)}):{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\displaystyle f_{i}^{(\rm x,t)}({\bf x},t)=f_{\rm mult}\left(f_{\rm swit,t}^{(1)}\left(t\right),f_{i}^{({\rm x})}({\bf x},t)\right)+f_{\rm mult}\left(f_{\rm swit,t}^{(2)}\left(t\right),\widetilde{f}_{i}^{(3)}({\bf x},t)\right),\quad i\in[D]

Combining with (

), we have

\begin{split}&\left|f_{i}^{(\rm x,t)}({\bf x},t)-(\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x}))_{i}\right|\leq\epsilon_{4}/\sigma_{t}+6m^{3DD_{1}^{2}}\delta(\log m)^{\tau_{\rm bd}}+4\delta\\
&\leq\epsilon_{4}/\sigma_{t}+10m^{3DD_{1}^{2}}\delta(\log m)^{\tau_{\rm bd}}\leq 11\epsilon_{4}/\sigma_{t},\quad i\in[D]\end{split}

(99)

for

7
implies that
\sigma_{t}\|\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x})\|_{\infty}\leq C_{S,2}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}

Combining with the last display, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{t}\left|f_{i}^{(\rm x,t)}({\bf x},t)\right|\leq 11\epsilon_{4}+C_{S,2}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}\leq D_{6}\sqrt{\log m},\quad i\in[D]

for

so that
\epsilon_{4}\leq\sqrt{\log m}
, where

Consider a function

\displaystyle f_{i}({\bf x},t)=\left(f_{i}^{(\rm x,t)}({\bf x},t)\vee-\sigma_{t}^{-1}D_{6}\sqrt{\log m}\right)\wedge\sigma_{t}^{-1}D_{6}\sqrt{\log m},\quad i\in[D]

),

\sigma_{t}\left\|{\bf f}({\bf x},t)-\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x})\right\|_{\infty}\leq 11\epsilon_{4}=11D_{5}m^{-\frac{\beta}{d}}(\log m)^{2D+2\beta}

(100)

for

B.5.5
Outside of Near-Support

Note that

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x})-{\bf f}({\bf x},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x})-{\bf f}({\bf x},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle+\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x})-{\bf f}({\bf x},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}

For
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}=\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf y})\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}{\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf y})\int_{\|\sigma_{t}{\bf z}+\mu_{t}{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf y}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf y})\sum_{i=1}^{D}\int_{|\sigma_{t}z_{i}+\mu_{t}y_{i}|\geq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf y}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf y})\sum_{i=1}^{D}\int_{|z_{i}|\geq D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf y}=D\int_{|z|\geq D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}\phi(z){\rm d}z,

where the last inequality holds because
|y_{i}|\leq 1
.
By the tail probability of the standard normal distribution, the last display is bounded by

2Dm^{-D_{1}^{2}/2}.

(101)

Let
C_{S,2}=C_{S,2}(D,K,\tau_{1},\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau})
be the constant in Lemma
7
.
Then,
\|\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x})\|_{2}\leq C_{S,2}(\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}-\mu_{t})/\sigma_{t}^{2}
for
\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}
with large enough
m
so that
D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}\geq 1
.
Combining with the last display, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}\|\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x})\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{S,2}^{2}\sigma_{t}^{-4}\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}\left(\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}^{2}+\mu_{t}^{2}\right)p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{S,2}^{2}\sigma_{t}^{-4}\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}\|{\bf x}\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}+2DC_{S,2}^{2}\sigma_{t}^{-4}\mu_{t}^{2}m^{-D_{1}^{2}/2}

for
t\geq 0
.
A simple calculation yields that

\displaystyle\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}\|{\bf x}\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{D}\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf y})\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}x_{i}^{2}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf x}{\rm d}{\bf y}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{D}\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf y})\int_{\|\sigma_{t}{\bf z}+\mu_{t}{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}(\sigma_{t}z_{i}+\mu_{t}y_{i})^{2}\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf y}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{D}\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf y})\int_{\|\sigma_{t}{\bf z}+\mu_{t}{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}2(\sigma_{t}^{2}z_{i}^{2}+\mu_{t}^{2}y_{i}^{2})\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf y}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{D}\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf y})\sum_{j=1}^{D}\int_{|\sigma_{t}z_{j}+\mu_{t}y_{j}|\geq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}2(\sigma_{t}^{2}z_{i}^{2}+\mu_{t}^{2}y_{i}^{2})\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf y}

for
t\geq 0
.
Since
|y_{j}|\leq 1
in the last integral, the last display is bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{D}\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf y})\sum_{j=1}^{D}\int_{|z_{j}|\geq D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}2(\sigma_{t}^{2}z_{i}^{2}+\mu_{t}^{2}y_{i}^{2})\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf y}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sigma_{t}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{j=1}^{D}\int_{|z_{j}|\geq D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}z_{i}^{2}\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}+4D^{2}\mu_{t}^{2}m^{-D_{1}^{2}/2},

where the last inequality holds by (
101
).
Furthermore,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{j=1}^{D}\int_{|z_{j}|\geq D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}z_{i}^{2}\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{D}\left\{(D-1){\mathbb{E}}[Z^{2}]\int_{|z|\geq D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}\phi(z){\rm d}z+\int_{|z|\geq D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}z^{2}\phi(z){\rm d}z\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2D\left\{(D-1)m^{-D_{1}^{2}/2}+\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}[Z^{4}]}m^{-D_{1}^{2}/4}\right\}\leq 2D(D+\sqrt{3}-1)m^{-D_{1}^{2}/4},

where the first inequality holds by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
Hence, there exists a constant
D_{7}=D_{7}(D,\underline{\tau},C_{S,2})
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}\|\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x})\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}\leq D_{7}\sigma_{t}^{-2}m^{-2\beta/d}

for
t\geq m^{-\tau_{\rm min}}
because
\mu_{t}\leq 1
and
\sigma_{t}\geq\sqrt{\underline{\tau}m^{-\tau_{\rm min}}}
.
Combining with (
101
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x})-{\bf f}({\bf x},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}2\|\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x})\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}+\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+\sigma_{t}D_{1}\sqrt{\log m}}2\|{\bf f}({\bf x},t)\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle\leq 2D_{7}\sigma_{t}^{-2}m^{-2\beta/d}+4\sigma_{t}^{-2}D^{2}D_{6}^{2}m^{-D_{1}^{2}/2}\log m

\displaystyle\leq\sigma_{t}^{-2}\left(2D_{7}m^{-2\beta/d}+4D^{2}D_{6}^{2}m^{-2\beta/d}\log m\right)

for
t\geq m^{-\tau_{\rm min}}
, where the second inequality holds because
\|{\bf f}({\bf x},t)\|_{\infty}\leq\sigma_{t}^{-1}D_{6}\sqrt{\log m}
for
{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D},t\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Combining with (
100
), we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{t}^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x})-{\bf f}({\bf x},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle\leq D11^{2}D_{5}^{2}m^{-\frac{2\beta}{d}}(\log m)^{4D+4\beta}+2D_{7}m^{-\frac{2\beta}{d}}+4D^{2}D_{6}^{2}m^{-D_{1}^{2}/2}\log m

\displaystyle\leq D_{8}m^{-\frac{2\beta}{d}}(\log m)^{4D+4\beta}

for
m^{-\tau_{\rm min}}\leq t\leq\tau_{\rm max}\log m
, where
D_{8}=D_{7}(D,D_{5},D_{6},D_{7})
.
Since
\sigma_{t}^{2}\geq\underline{\tau}t
for
t\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\left\|\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x})-{\bf f}({\bf x},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq D_{8}\underline{\tau}^{-1}m^{-\frac{2\beta}{d}}(\log m)^{4D+4\beta}\left(\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T}\right)

\displaystyle\leq D_{9}m^{-\frac{2\beta}{d}}(\log m)^{4D+4\beta+1},

where
D_{9}=D_{9}(\underline{\tau},\tau_{\rm max},\tau_{\rm min},D_{8})
.
Lemma
9
, Lemma
10
, Lemma
11
, Lemma
12
and Lemma
13
imply that
{\bf f}^{(\rm x,t)}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm WSNN}(L,{\bf d},s,m,M,\mathcal{P})
with

\displaystyle L\leq D_{10}(\log m)^{6}\log\log m,\quad\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}\leq D_{10}m^{D+1},

\displaystyle s\leq D_{10}m(\log m)^{5}\log\log m,\quad M\leq\exp(D_{10}\{\log m\}^{6}),

\|{\bf m}\|_{\infty}\leq D_{10}m^{D}
and the set of permutation matrices
\mathcal{P}
,
where

\displaystyle D_{10}=D_{10}(\beta,d,D,\tau_{\rm min},\widetilde{C}_{3},\widetilde{C}_{6},\widetilde{C}_{9},C_{N,1},C_{N,4},C_{N,5},C_{S,1},D_{1})

is a large enough constant.
The assertion follows by re-defining the constants.

Appendix C
Proofs for the Convergence Rate

In this section, we provide the proof of Theorem
3
.
We begin by outlining auxiliary lemmas and a proposition.

Lemma 26

(Error bound for small
t
)
.

Let
\beta,K>0
be given and suppose the true density
p_{0}
belongs to
\mathcal{H}^{\beta,K}([-1,1]^{D})
.
Then, there exist positive constants
\widetilde{C}_{12}=\widetilde{C}_{12}(\beta,D,K,\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau})
and
\widetilde{C}_{13}=\widetilde{C}_{13}(\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau})
such that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\left|p_{0}({\bf x})-p_{t}({\bf x})\right|{\rm d}{\bf x}\leq\widetilde{C}_{12}\left\{t\log(1/t)\right\}^{\frac{\beta\wedge 1}{2}}

for
0<t\leq\widetilde{C}_{13}
.

For any function
{\bf f}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{1}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{2}},n_{1},n_{2}\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
C>0
, denote
\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}([-C,C]^{n_{1}})}
as the sup-norm over
[-C,C]^{n_{1}}
, defined as

\displaystyle\|{\bf f}\|_{L^{\infty}([-C,C]^{n_{1}})}=\sup_{{\bf x}\in[-C,C]^{n_{1}}}\|{\bf f}({\bf x})\|_{\infty}.

For a function space
\mathcal{F}
,
N(\delta,\mathcal{F},d)
denotes the covering numbers with respect to the (pseudo)-metric
d
; see
van der Vaart and Wellner (1996)
for the detailed definition.
The following lemma provides a covering number of
\mathcal{F}_{\rm WSNN}
.
Our main proof strategy follows the proof of Lemma 5 from
Schmidt-Hieber (2020)
, with modifications for weight-sharing networks.

Lemma 27

(Covering number of
\mathcal{F}_{\rm WSNN}
)
.

Let
C
and
0<\delta<1
be given.
For the class of weight-sharing neural networks
\mathcal{F}_{\rm WSNN}(L,{\bf d},s,M,\mathcal{P}_{{\bf m}})
,
we have

\displaystyle\log N\left(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\rm WSNN}(L,{\bf d},s,M,\mathcal{P}_{{\bf m}}),\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}([-C,C]^{d_{1}})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(s+1)\log\left(\frac{4L^{2}\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}^{2}\left\{\|{\bf m}\|_{\infty}\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}(M\vee 1)\right\}^{L}\left(L+C+3\right)}{\delta}\right).

For
\underline{T},\overline{T}>0
with
\underline{T}<\overline{T}
and a function
{\bf f}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
, recall the definition of loss function
\ell_{{\bf f}}:[-1,1]^{D}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
, given by

\displaystyle\ell_{{\bf f}}({\bf x})

\displaystyle=\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\Big\|{\bf f}({\bf X}_{t},t)+\frac{{\bf X}_{t}-\mu_{t}{\bf X}_{0}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\Big\|_{2}^{2}~\big|~{\bf X}_{0}={\bf x}\Big]{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\Big\|{\bf f}(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)+\frac{{\bf Z}}{\sigma_{t}}\Big\|_{2}^{2}\Big]{\rm d}t,

where the last equality holds because
{\bf X}_{t}=\mu_{t}{\bf X}_{0}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z}
with
D
-dimensional standard normal variable
{\bf Z}
.
Define the pointwise excess risk by

\displaystyle\nu_{{\bf f}}({\bf x})=\ell_{{\bf f}}({\bf x})-\ell_{{\bf f}_{0}}({\bf x}),

where
({\bf x},t)\mapsto{\bf f}_{0}({\bf x},t)=\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x})
is a score function.
Combining with (
5
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\nu_{{\bf f}}({\bf X}_{0})]=\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\bf f}({\bf X}_{t},t)-{\bf f}_{0}({\bf X}_{t},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]{\rm d}t.

The following lemma provides an upper bound on the sup-norm and the second moment (hence a variance-type bound) of the excess risk over the bounded function class.

Lemma 28

(Upper bounds of excess risk)
.

Let
K,\tau_{1},F,\underline{T},\overline{T}>0
be given and suppose the true density
p_{0}
satisfies that
\tau_{1}\leq p_{0}({\bf x})\leq K
for any
{\bf x}\in[-1,1]^{D}
.
For any function
{\bf f}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
satisfying

\|{\bf f}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{D})}\leq F\sigma_{t}^{-1}
for all
t\in[\underline{T},\overline{T}]
, there exists a positive constant
\widetilde{C}_{14}=\widetilde{C}_{14}(D,K,\tau_{1},\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau})
such that

\displaystyle\|\nu_{{\bf f}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1]^{D})}\leq\widetilde{C}_{14}(F^{2}\vee 1)\left(\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T}\right)

and

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\{\nu_{{\bf f}}({\bf X}_{0})\right\}^{2}\right]\leq 2\widetilde{C}_{14}(F^{2}\vee 1)\left(\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\nu_{{\bf f}}({\bf X}_{0})\right].

The following proposition provides an oracle inequality for the ERM estimator under the score-matching loss.
The statement is similar to Theorem C.4 from
Oko et al. (2023)
while addressing the issue identified by
Yakovlev and Puchkin (2025)
.
Our main proof strategy follows the proof of Proposition 12 from
Stéphanovitch et al. (2025)
with modifications that control the excess risk variance.

Proposition 29

(Oracle inequality for score matching)
.

Let
K,\tau_{1},F,\underline{T},\overline{T}>0
be given and suppose the true density
p_{0}
satisfies that
\tau_{1}\leq p_{0}({\bf x})\leq K
for any
{\bf x}\in[-1,1]^{D}
.
Let
{\bf X}^{1},\ldots,{\bf X}^{n}
be the
i.i.d.
samples drawn from
p_{0}
.
For the class
\mathcal{F}
, consisting of continuous functions
{\bf f}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
with
\|{\bf f}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{D})}\leq F\sigma_{t}^{-1}
for all
t\in[\underline{T},\overline{T}]
, let

\displaystyle\widehat{\bf f}\in\argmin_{{\bf f}\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell_{{\bf f}}({\bf X}^{i})

be the ERM estimator over the class
\mathcal{F}
.
Then, there exists a positive constant
\widetilde{C}_{14}=\widetilde{C}_{14}(D,K,\tau_{1},\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau})
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\widehat{\bf f}({\bf X}_{t},t)-{\bf f}_{0}({\bf X}_{t},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq 3\inf_{{\bf f}\in\mathcal{F}}\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\bf f}({\bf X}_{t},t)-{\bf f}_{0}({\bf X}_{t},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\overline{C}_{1}}{n}\left\{\log N\left(n^{-2},\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}([-\overline{C}_{2},\overline{C}_{2}]^{D+1})}\right)+\log(2n)\right\},

where

\displaystyle\overline{C}_{1}=\widetilde{C}_{14}(F^{2}\vee 1)\left(\sqrt{\overline{T}}+\sqrt{\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T}}\right)\left(\sqrt{\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T}}\right)

and

\displaystyle\overline{C}_{2}=\left(1+2\sqrt{2\log n}\right)\vee\overline{T}.

C.1
Proof of Lemma
26

Proof.

Note that
p_{0}
is continuous and supported on
[-1,1]^{D}
.
Thus, for
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\left|p_{0}({\bf x})-p_{t}({\bf x})\right|{\rm d}{\bf x}=\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\left|p_{0}({\bf x})-p_{t}({\bf x})\right|{\rm d}{\bf x}+\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq 1}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}.

We will derive error bounds for each integral on the RHS.

Note that
p_{0}\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta,K}([-1,1]^{D})
.
If
\beta\leq 1
,
|p_{0}({\bf x})-p_{0}({\bf y})|\leq K\|{\bf x}-{\bf y}\|_{\infty}^{\beta}
for any
{\bf x},{\bf y}\in[-1,1]^{D}
by the definition of
\mathcal{H}^{\beta,K}
.
If
\beta>1
, Mean value theorem implies that

|p_{0}({\bf x})-p_{0}({\bf y})|\leq K\|{\bf x}-{\bf y}\|_{2}
for any
{\bf x},{\bf y}\in[-1,1]^{D}
.
Combining two cases, we have

|p_{0}({\bf x})-p_{0}({\bf y})|\leq K\sqrt{D}\|{\bf x}-{\bf y}\|_{\infty}^{\beta\wedge 1},\quad\forall{\bf x},{\bf y}\in[-1,1]^{D}.

(102)

A simple calculation yields that for any
{\bf x}\in[-1,1]^{D}
,

\displaystyle p_{0}({\bf x})-p_{t}({\bf x})

\displaystyle=\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\left\{p_{0}({\bf x})-p_{0}({\bf y})\right\}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}+p_{0}({\bf x})\left(1-\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}\right).

Then, for any
t\geq 0
,

\begin{split}&\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\left|p_{0}({\bf x})-p_{t}({\bf x})\right|{\rm d}{\bf x}\\
&\leq\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\left\{p_{0}({\bf x})-p_{0}({\bf y})\right\}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}\right|{\rm d}{\bf x}\\
&\quad+\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf x})\left|1-\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}\right|{\rm d}{\bf x}\end{split}

(103)

We bound each term on the RHS.
For any
\delta>0
, a simple calculation yields that

\begin{split}&\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\left\{p_{0}({\bf x})-p_{0}({\bf y})\right\}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}\right|{\rm d}{\bf x}\\
&\leq K\sqrt{D}\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\|{\bf x}-{\bf y}\|_{\infty}^{\beta\wedge 1}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}{\rm d}{\bf x}\\
&\leq K\sqrt{D}\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\left(\delta^{\beta\wedge 1}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\\
\|{\bf x}-{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq\delta\end{subarray}}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}+2^{\beta\wedge 1}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\\
\|{\bf x}-{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\geq\delta\end{subarray}}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}\right){\rm d}{\bf x}\\
&\leq K\sqrt{D}2^{D}\mu_{t}^{-D}\delta^{\beta\wedge 1}+K\sqrt{D}2^{\beta\wedge 1}\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\\
\|{\bf x}-{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\geq\delta\end{subarray}}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}{\rm d}{\bf x},\end{split}

(104)

where the first inequality holds by (
102
) and the last inequality holds because

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}=\mu_{t}^{-D},\quad\forall{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}.

(105)

Since
1-\mu_{t}\geq 0
, we have
\|{\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\geq\|{\bf x}-{\bf y}\|_{\infty}-(1-\mu_{t})\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\geq\delta-(1-\mu_{t})
for
\|{\bf x}-{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\geq\delta
and
\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
.
Then, a simple calculation yields that

\displaystyle\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\\
\|{\bf x}-{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\geq\delta\end{subarray}}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}{\rm d}{\bf x}\leq\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\int_{\|{\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\geq\delta-(1-\mu_{t})}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}{\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle=\mu_{t}^{-D}\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\int_{\|{\bf z}\|_{\infty}\geq\frac{\delta-(1-\mu_{t})}{\sigma_{t}}}\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf x}=2^{D}\mu_{t}^{-D}\int_{\|{\bf z}\|_{\infty}\geq\frac{\delta-(1-\mu_{t})}{\sigma_{t}}}\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{D}\mu_{t}^{-D}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\int_{|z_{i}|\geq\frac{\delta-(1-\mu_{t})}{\sigma_{t}}}\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}\leq D2^{D+1}\mu_{t}^{-D}\exp\left(-\frac{(\delta-(1-\mu_{t}))^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)

for
\delta\geq 1-\mu_{t}
, where the last inequality holds by the tail probability of the standard normal distribution.
By (
27
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\leq 1-\overline{\tau}t\leq\mu_{t}\leq 1-\frac{\underline{\tau}t}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\sqrt{\underline{\tau}t}\leq\sigma_{t}\leq\sqrt{2\overline{\tau}t}

for any
0\leq t\leq(2\overline{\tau})^{-1}
.
Let
\delta=1-\mu_{t}+2\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}
.
Combining last display with (
104
), a simple calculation yields that

\begin{split}&\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\left\{p_{0}({\bf x})-p_{0}({\bf y})\right\}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}\right|{\rm d}{\bf x}\\
&\leq K\sqrt{D}2^{D}\mu_{t}^{-D}\delta^{\beta\wedge 1}+KD\sqrt{D}2^{D+1+\beta\wedge 1}\mu_{t}^{-D}\exp\left(-\frac{(\delta-(1-\mu_{t}))^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\\
&\leq K\sqrt{D}2^{D+1}\left(\overline{\tau}t+2\sqrt{2\overline{\tau}t\log(1/\sqrt{\underline{\tau}t})}\right)^{\beta\wedge 1}+KD\sqrt{D}2^{2D+1+\beta\wedge 1}\left(\sqrt{2\overline{\tau}t}\right)^{2}\\
&\leq D_{1}\left\{t\log(1/t)\right\}^{\frac{\beta\wedge 1}{2}}\end{split}

(106)

for any
0\leq t\leq(2\overline{\tau})^{-1}\wedge 1
,
where
D_{1}=D_{1}(\beta,D,K,\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau})
.

For any
{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
, we have

\displaystyle 1-\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}

\displaystyle=1-\mu_{t}^{-D}+\mu_{t}^{-D}-\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}

\displaystyle=1-\mu_{t}^{-D}+\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\geq 1}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y},

where the last equality holds by (
105
).
A simple calculation yields that

\displaystyle|1-\mu_{t}^{-D}|=\mu_{t}^{-D}|\mu_{t}^{D}-1|=\mu_{t}^{-D}|1-\mu_{t}|\sum_{k=0}^{D-1}\mu_{t}^{k}\leq D2^{D}|1-\mu_{t}|\leq D2^{D}\overline{\tau}t

for any
0\leq t\leq(2\overline{\tau})^{-1}
.
For
\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu_{t}-2\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}
and
\|\sigma_{t}{\bf z}+{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}
, we have
\|\sigma_{t}{\bf z}\|_{\infty}\geq\|\sigma_{t}{\bf z}+{\bf x}\|_{\infty}-\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq 2\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\geq 1}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}=\mu_{t}^{-D}\int_{\|\sigma_{t}{\bf z}+{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}}\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}\leq\mu_{t}^{-D}\int_{\|{\bf z}\|_{\infty}\geq 2\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}}\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}.

By the tail probability of the standard normal distribution, the last display is bounded by

\displaystyle\mu_{t}^{-D}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\int_{|z_{i}|\geq 2\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}}\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}\leq 2D\mu_{t}^{-D}\sigma_{t}^{2}\leq 2^{D+2}D\overline{\tau}t

for any
0\leq t\leq(2\overline{\tau})^{-1}\wedge 1
.
Then,

\displaystyle\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf x})\left|1-\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}\right|{\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle\leq D2^{D}\overline{\tau}t+\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf x})\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\geq 1}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}{\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle\leq D2^{D}\overline{\tau}t+2^{D+2}D\overline{\tau}t+\int_{\mu_{t}-2\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}\leq\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf x})\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\geq 1}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}{\rm d}{\bf x}.

A simple telescoping sum implies that

\displaystyle 2^{D}-\left(2\mu_{t}-4\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}\right)^{D}

\displaystyle=\left(2-2\mu_{t}+4\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}\right)\sum_{k=0}^{D-1}2^{D-1-k}\left(2\mu_{t}-4\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}\right)^{k}

\displaystyle\leq\left(2\overline{\tau}t+4\sqrt{2\overline{\tau}t\log(1/\sqrt{\underline{\tau}t})}\right)2^{D-1}D

for
0\leq t\leq D_{2}
, where
D_{2}=D_{2}(\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau})
is a small enough constant so that
D_{2}\leq(2\overline{\tau})^{-1}\wedge 1
and
2\mu_{t}-4\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}\leq 2
for
t\leq D_{2}
.
Combining (
105
) with the last two displays, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf x})\left|1-\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}\right|{\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle\leq D2^{D}\mu_{t}^{-D}\overline{\tau}t+2^{D+2}D\overline{\tau}t+KD2^{D}\left(\overline{\tau}t+2\sqrt{2\overline{\tau}t\log(1/\sqrt{\underline{\tau}t}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq D_{3}\sqrt{t\log(1/t)}

for
0\leq t\leq D_{2}
, where
D_{3}=D_{3}(D,K,\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau}).

Combinig (
103
) and (
106
) with the last display, we have

\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\left|p_{0}({\bf x})-p_{t}({\bf x})\right|{\rm d}{\bf x}\leq D_{1}\left\{t\log(1/t)\right\}^{\frac{\beta\wedge 1}{2}}+D_{3}\sqrt{t\log(1/t)}

(107)

for
0\leq t\leq D_{2}
.

Note that

\displaystyle\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq 1}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}\leq\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+2\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}+\int_{1\leq\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu_{t}+2\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}

and we bound each term on the RHS.
For
\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+2\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}
and
\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
,
we have
\|{\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\geq\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}-\mu_{t}\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\geq 2\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}
.

Then,

\displaystyle\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+2\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}=\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+2\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}}\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf y})\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}{\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf y})\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq\mu_{t}+2\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf x}{\rm d}{\bf y}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf y})\int_{\|{\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\geq 2\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf x}{\rm d}{\bf y}

\displaystyle=\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf y})\int_{\|{\bf z}\|_{\infty}\geq 2\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}}\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf y}=\int_{\|{\bf z}\|_{\infty}\geq 2\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}}\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{D}\int_{|z_{i}|\geq 2\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}}\phi_{1}({\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}\leq 2D\sigma_{t}^{2}\leq 4D\overline{\tau}t,\quad\forall 0\leq t\leq(2\overline{\tau})^{-1},

where the third inequality holds by the tail probability of the standard normal distribution.
A simple calculation yields that

\displaystyle\int_{1\leq\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu_{t}+2\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle=\int_{1\leq\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu_{t}+2\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}}\int_{\|{\bf y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}p_{0}({\bf y})\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}{\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{1\leq\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq\mu_{t}+2\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}}K\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\phi_{\sigma_{t}}({\bf x}-\mu_{t}{\bf y}){\rm d}{\bf y}{\rm d}{\bf x}=K\mu_{t}^{-D}\left|\left(2\mu_{t}+4\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}\right)^{D}-2^{D}\right|

\displaystyle\leq K\mu_{t}^{-D}\left(|2\mu_{t}-2|+4\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}\right)\sum_{k=0}^{D-1}\left(2\mu_{t}+4\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}\right)^{{D-1-k}}2^{k}

\displaystyle\leq K2^{D}\left(2\overline{\tau}t+4\sqrt{2\overline{\tau}t\log(1/\sqrt{\underline{\tau}t})}\right)D4^{D}

for
0\leq t\leq D_{4}
, where
D_{4}=D_{4}(\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau})
is a small enough constant so that
D_{4}\leq D_{2}
and
\mu_{t}+2\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log(1/\sigma_{t})}\leq 2
for
t\leq D_{4}
.
Combining with the last two displays, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq 1}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}\leq D_{5}\sqrt{t\log(1/t)}

for
0\leq t\leq D_{4}
, where
D_{5}=D_{5}(D,K,\overline{\tau})
.
Therefore, combining (
107
) with the last display,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\left|p_{0}({\bf x})-p_{t}({\bf x})\right|{\rm d}{\bf x}=\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\left|p_{0}({\bf x})-p_{t}({\bf x})\right|{\rm d}{\bf x}+\int_{\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}\geq 1}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle\leq D_{1}\left\{t\log(1/t)\right\}^{\frac{\beta\wedge 1}{2}}+D_{3}\sqrt{t\log(1/t)}+D_{5}\sqrt{t\log(1/t)}\leq D_{6}\left\{t\log(1/t)\right\}^{\frac{\beta\wedge 1}{2}}

where
D_{6}=D_{1}+D_{3}+D_{5}
.
The assertion follows by redefining the constants.

∎

C.2
Proof of Lemma
27

Proof.

For neural networks
{\bf f},\widetilde{\bf f}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm WSNN}(L,{\bf d},s,M,\mathcal{P}_{{\bf m}})
, let
\{W_{l},{\bf b}_{l}\}_{l\in[L]}
and
\{\widetilde{W}_{l},\widetilde{\bf b}_{l}\}_{l\in[L]}
be the parameter matrices of
{\bf f}
and
\widetilde{\bf f}
, repsectively.
For
l\in[L-1]
, let

\displaystyle{\bf f}_{l}(\cdot)=\rho\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m_{l}}R_{l}^{(j)}\left(W_{l}Q_{l}^{(j)}\cdot+{\bf b}_{l}\right)\right)\text{\quad and \quad}\widetilde{\bf f}_{l}(\cdot)=\rho\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m_{l}}R_{l}^{(j)}\left(\widetilde{W}_{l}Q_{l}^{(j)}\cdot+\widetilde{\bf b}_{l}\right)\right).

Given
\epsilon>0
, assume that all parameter values of
{\bf f}
and
\widetilde{\bf f}
are at most
\epsilon
away from each other.
Then,

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf f}_{l}({\bf x})-{\bf f}_{l}(\widetilde{\bf x})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{m_{l}}R_{l}^{(j)}W_{l}Q_{l}^{(j)}({\bf x}-\widetilde{\bf x})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m_{l}}\left\|W_{l}Q_{l}^{(j)}\left({\bf x}-\widetilde{\bf x}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m_{l}}\left\{d_{l}\left\|W_{l}\right\|_{\infty}\cdot\left\|Q_{l}^{(j)}\left({\bf x}-\widetilde{\bf x}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq m_{l}d_{l}M\|{\bf x}-\widetilde{\bf x}\|_{\infty},\quad l\in[L-1],\ {\bf x},\widetilde{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{l}}.

because for
{\bf z}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{2}}
,
n_{1},n_{2}\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we have
\|W{\bf z}\|_{\infty}\leq n_{2}\|W\|_{\infty}\|{\bf z}\|_{\infty}
for any matrix
W\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n_{1}\times n_{2}}
and
\|Q{\bf z}\|_{\infty}=\|{\bf z}\|_{\infty}
for any
n_{1}\times n_{1}
permutation matrix
Q
.
A simple calculation yields that

\begin{split}&\left\|\left({\bf f}_{L-1}\circ\cdots\circ{\bf f}_{l}\right)({\bf x})-\left({\bf f}_{L-1}\circ\cdots\circ{\bf f}_{l}\right)(\widetilde{\bf x})\right\|_{\infty}\\
&\leq\left(\prod_{i=l}^{L-1}m_{i}d_{i}M\right)\|{\bf x}-\widetilde{\bf x}\|_{\infty},\quad l\in[L-1],{\bf x},\widetilde{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{l}}.\end{split}

(108)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf f}_{l}({\bf x})-\widetilde{\bf f}_{l}({\bf x})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{m_{l}}R_{l}^{(j)}\left(\left(W_{l}-\widetilde{W}_{l}\right)Q_{l}^{(j)}{\bf x}+{\bf b}_{l}-\widetilde{\bf b}_{l}\right)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m_{l}}\left\|\left(W_{l}-\widetilde{W}_{l}\right)Q_{l}^{(j)}{\bf x}+\left({\bf b}_{l}-\widetilde{\bf b}_{l}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m_{l}}\left\{\left\|W_{l}-\widetilde{W}_{l}\right\|_{\infty}\cdot\left\|Q_{l}^{(j)}{\bf x}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|{\bf b}_{l}-\widetilde{\bf b}_{l}\right\|_{\infty}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq m_{l}\epsilon(1+d_{l}\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty})\leq m_{l}d_{l}\epsilon(1+\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}),\quad l\in[L-1],\ {\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{l}}.

Note also that

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\bf f}_{l}({\bf x})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{m_{l}}\left\|R_{l}^{(i)}\left(\widetilde{W}_{l}Q_{l}^{(i)}{\bf x}+\widetilde{\bf b}_{l}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\leq m_{l}d_{l}M\left(\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}+1\right)

and

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\widetilde{\bf f}_{l}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{\bf f}_{1}\right)({\bf x})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\prod_{i=1}^{l}m_{i}d_{i}M\right)\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}+\sum_{k=0}^{l-1}\left(\prod_{i=k+1}^{l}m_{i}d_{i}M\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}+l\right)\prod_{i=1}^{l}\{m_{i}d_{i}(M\vee 1)\}.

It follows that

\begin{split}&\left\|\left({\bf f}_{l+1}\circ\widetilde{\bf f}_{l}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{\bf f}_{1}\right)({\bf x})-\left(\widetilde{\bf f}_{l+1}\circ\widetilde{\bf f}_{l}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{\bf f}_{1}\right)({\bf x})\right\|_{\infty}\\
&\leq m_{l+1}d_{l+1}\epsilon\left\{1+\left\|\left(\widetilde{\bf f}_{l}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{\bf f}_{1}\right)({\bf x})\right\|_{\infty}\right\}\\
&\leq m_{l+1}d_{l+1}\epsilon\left[1+\left(\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}+l\right)\prod_{i=1}^{l}\{m_{i}d_{i}(M\vee 1)\}\right]\\
&\leq\epsilon(M\vee 1)^{-1}\left(\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}+l+1\right)\prod_{i=1}^{l+1}\left\{m_{i}d_{i}(M\vee 1)\right\},\quad l\in[L-2],\ {\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}.\end{split}

(109)

Let

\displaystyle{\bf f}^{(l)}(\cdot)=\left({\bf f}_{L-1}\circ\cdots\circ{\bf f}_{l+1}\circ\widetilde{\bf f}_{l}\circ\cdots\widetilde{\bf f}_{1}\right)(\cdot),\quad l\in[L-2]

\displaystyle{\bf f}^{(0)}(\cdot)=\left({\bf f}_{L-1}\circ\cdots\circ{\bf f}_{1}\right)(\cdot)\text{\quad and \quad}{\bf f}^{(L-1)}(\cdot)=\left(\widetilde{\bf f}_{L-1}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{\bf f}_{1}\right)(\cdot).

Combining with (
108
) and (
109
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf f}^{(l)}({\bf x})-{\bf f}^{(l+1)}({\bf x})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left({\bf f}_{L-1}\circ\cdots\circ{\bf f}_{l+1}\circ\widetilde{\bf f}_{l}\circ\cdots\widetilde{\bf f}_{1}\right)({\bf x})-\left({\bf f}_{L-1}\circ\cdots\circ{\bf f}_{l+2}\circ\widetilde{\bf f}_{l+1}\circ\cdots\widetilde{\bf f}_{1}\right)({\bf x})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\left(\prod_{i=l+2}^{L-1}m_{i}d_{i}M\right)(M\vee 1)^{-1}\left(\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}+l+1\right)\prod_{i=1}^{l+1}\left\{m_{i}d_{i}(M\vee 1)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon(M\vee 1)^{-1}\left(\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}+l+1\right)\prod_{i=1}^{L-1}\left\{m_{i}d_{i}(M\vee 1)\right\},\quad l\in[L-3],{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}

and

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf f}^{(L-2)}({\bf x})-{\bf f}^{(L-1)}({\bf x})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left({\bf f}_{L-1}\circ\widetilde{\bf f}_{L-2}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{\bf f}_{1}\right)({\bf x})-\left(\widetilde{\bf f}_{L-1}\circ\cdots\circ\widetilde{\bf f}_{1}\right)({\bf x})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq m_{L-1}d_{L-1}\epsilon\left[1+\left(\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}+L-2\right)\prod_{i=1}^{L-2}\{m_{i}d_{i}(M\vee 1)\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon(M\vee 1)^{-1}\left(\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}+L-1\right)\prod_{i=1}^{L-1}\left\{m_{i}d_{i}(M\vee 1)\right\},\quad{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}.

A simple calculation yields that

\displaystyle\left\|{\bf f}({\bf x})-\widetilde{\bf f}({\bf x})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{l=0}^{L-2}\left\|W_{L}\left({\bf f}^{(l)}({\bf x})-{\bf f}^{(l+1)}({\bf x})\right)\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\left(W_{L}-\widetilde{W}_{L}\right){\bf f}^{(L-1)}({\bf x})-\left({\bf b}_{L}-\widetilde{\bf b}_{L}\right)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq d_{L}\left\|W_{L}\right\|_{\infty}\sum_{l=0}^{L-2}\left\|{\bf f}^{(l)}({\bf x})-{\bf f}^{(l+1)}({\bf x})\right\|_{\infty}+d_{L}\left\|W_{L}-\widetilde{W}_{L}\right\|_{\infty}\cdot\left\|{\bf f}^{(L-1)}({\bf x})\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|{\bf b}_{L}-\widetilde{\bf b}_{L}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon d_{L}M(M\vee 1)^{-1}\left\{\sum_{l=0}^{L-2}(l+1+\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty})\right\}\prod_{i=1}^{L-1}\left\{m_{i}d_{i}(M\vee 1)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon d_{L}\left(\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}+L-1\right)\prod_{i=1}^{L-1}\{m_{i}d_{i}(M\vee 1)\}+\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon d_{L}\left\{\frac{L(L+1)}{2}+L\|{\bf x}\|_{\infty}+2\right\}\prod_{i=1}^{L-1}\left\{m_{i}d_{i}(M\vee 1)\right\},\quad{\bf x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}.

For
{\bf x}\in[-C,C]^{d_{1}}
, the last display is bounded by

\displaystyle\epsilon d_{L}L\left(L+C+3\right)\prod_{i=1}^{L-1}\left\{m_{i}d_{i}(M\vee 1)\right\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}\delta.

The total number of parameters in
{\bf f}
is
T\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm def}}{{=}}\sum_{i=1}^{L}(d_{i}+1)d_{i+1}
and there are
\binom{T}{s}
combinations to pick
s
non-zero paramters.
Since
s\leq T\leq 2L\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}^{2}
and
\binom{T}{s}\leq T{{}^{s}}\leq(2L\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}^{2})^{s}
, we have

\displaystyle N\left(\delta,\mathcal{F}_{\rm WSNN}(L,{\bf d},s,M,\mathcal{P}_{{\bf m}}),\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}[-C,C]^{d_{1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s_{0}=1}^{s}\binom{T}{s_{0}}N\left(\epsilon,[-M,M]^{s_{0}},\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\leq s\left(2L\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}^{2}\right)^{s}\left(\frac{2M}{\epsilon}\vee 1\right)^{s}

\displaystyle\leq\left(2L\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}^{2}\right)^{s+1}\left(\frac{2MLd_{L}\left(L+C+3\right)\prod_{i=1}^{L-1}\left\{m_{i}d_{i}(M\vee 1)\right\}}{\delta}\vee 1\right)^{s}.

For
\delta<1
, the last display is bounded by

\displaystyle\left(\frac{4L^{2}\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}^{2}\left\{\|{\bf m}\|_{\infty}\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}(M\vee 1)\right\}^{L}\left(L+C+3\right)}{\delta}\right)^{s+1}.

The assertion follows by taking the logarithm.
∎

C.3
Proof of Lemma
28

Proof.

For any
{\bf x}\in[-1,1]^{D}
, we have

\displaystyle\ell_{{\bf f}}({\bf x})\leq\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}2{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\bf f}(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]+\frac{2{\mathbb{E}}[\|{\bf Z}\|_{2}^{2}]}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\rm d}t\leq\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\frac{2DF^{2}+2D}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\rm d}t,

where the last inequality holds because
\|{\bf f}({\bf x},t)\|_{\infty}\leq F\sigma_{t}^{-1}
.
Since
\sigma_{t}\geq\sqrt{\underline{\tau}t}
for all
t\geq 0
by (
27
), we have

|\ell_{{\bf f}}({\bf x})|\leq\underline{\tau}^{-1}2D(F^{2}+1)(\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T}).

(110)

Lemma
7
implies that there exists a constant
C_{S,2}=C_{S,2}(D,K,\tau_{1},\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau})>0
such that

\displaystyle\|{\bf f}_{0}(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf z},t)\|_{2}\leq\frac{C_{S,2}}{\sigma_{t}}\left(\|{\bf z}\|_{\infty}\vee 1\right)

for any
{\bf x}\in[-1,1]^{D}
and
{\bf z}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\ell_{{\bf f}_{0}}({\bf x})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}2{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\bf f}_{0}(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]+\frac{2{\mathbb{E}}[\|{\bf Z}\|_{2}^{2}]}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\frac{2C_{S,2}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[\left(\|{\bf Z}\|_{\infty}\vee 1\right)^{2}]+2D}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\rm d}t.

Since
(\|{\bf z}\|_{\infty}\vee 1)^{2}\leq(\|{\bf z}\|_{\infty}+1)^{2}\leq(\|{\bf z}\|_{2}+1)^{2}\leq 2\|{\bf z}\|_{2}^{2}+2
, the last display is bounded by

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\frac{2C_{S,2}^{2}(2D+2)+2D}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\rm d}t\leq\underline{\tau}^{-1}\left\{2C_{S,2}^{2}(2D+2)+2D\right\}(\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T}).

Combining the last display with (
110
), we have

\displaystyle|\nu_{{\bf f}}({\bf x})|=|\ell_{{\bf f}}({\bf x})-\ell_{{\bf f}_{0}}({\bf x})|\leq\ell_{{\bf f}}({\bf x})+\ell_{{\bf f}_{0}}({\bf x})\leq D_{1}(F^{2}\vee 1)(\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T}),

where
D_{1}=D_{1}(D,\underline{\tau},C_{S,2})
. The first assertion follows by redefining the constant.

Simple calculation yields that

\displaystyle|\nu_{{\bf f}}({\bf x})|=|\ell_{{\bf f}}({\bf x})-\ell_{{\bf f}_{0}}({\bf x})|=\left|{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\left\|{\bf f}(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)+\frac{{\bf Z}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|{\bf f}_{0}(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)+\frac{{\bf Z}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}{\rm d}t\right]\right|

\displaystyle=\left|{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\left\{({\bf f}-{\bf f}_{0})(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)\right\}^{\top}\left\{({\bf f}+{\bf f}_{0})(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)+\frac{2{\bf Z}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\}{\rm d}t\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sqrt{\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\left\|({\bf f}-{\bf f}_{0})(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}{\rm d}t}\sqrt{\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\left\|({\bf f}+{\bf f}_{0})(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)+\frac{2{\bf Z}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}{\rm d}t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\left\|({\bf f}-{\bf f}_{0})(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}{\rm d}t\right]}\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\left\|({\bf f}+{\bf f}_{0})(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)+\frac{2{\bf Z}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}{\rm d}t\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\left\|({\bf f}-{\bf f}_{0})(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}{\rm d}t\right]}\sqrt{2\ell_{{\bf f}}({\bf x})+2\ell_{{\bf f}_{0}}({\bf x})},

where the first and second inequalities hold by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
Combining with the last two displays, the second assertion follows by

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\{\nu_{{\bf f}}({\bf X}_{0})\right\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(2\ell_{{\bf f}}({\bf X}_{0})+2\ell_{{\bf f}_{0}}({\bf X}_{0})\right)\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\left\|({\bf f}-{\bf f}_{0})(\mu_{t}{\bf X}_{0}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}{\rm d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2D_{1}(F^{2}\vee 1)(\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T})\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\bf f}({\bf X}_{t},t)-{\bf f}_{0}({\bf X}_{t},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]{\rm d}t.

∎

C.4
Proof of Proposition
29

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be the class of functions
{\bf f}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
satisfying
\|{\bf f}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{D})}\leq F\sigma_{t}^{-1}
for all
t\in[\underline{T},\overline{T}]
.
Let
\mathcal{V}=\{\nu_{{\bf f}}(\cdot):{\bf f}\in\mathcal{F}\}
.
For
\nu\in\mathcal{V}
, denote

\displaystyle R(\nu)=\int_{[-1,1]^{D}}\nu({\bf x})p_{0}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}\text{\quad and \quad}\widehat{R}_{n}(\nu)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\nu({\bf X}^{i}).

Moreover, let

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nu}\in\argmin_{\nu\in\mathcal{V}}R(\nu)\text{\quad and \quad}\widehat{\nu}\in\argmin_{\nu\in\mathcal{V}}\widehat{R}_{n}(\nu).

Note

\begin{split}R(\widehat{\nu})&=R(\widehat{\nu})-\widehat{R}_{n}(\widehat{\nu})+\widehat{R}_{n}(\widehat{\nu})\\
&\leq R(\widehat{\nu})-\widehat{R}_{n}(\widehat{\nu})+\widehat{R}_{n}(\widetilde{\nu})\\
&=R(\widetilde{\nu})+\left\{R(\widehat{\nu})-\widehat{R}_{n}(\widehat{\nu})\right\}+\left\{\widehat{R}_{n}(\widetilde{\nu})-R(\widetilde{\nu})\right\},\end{split}

(111)

where the inequality holds because
\widehat{\nu}
is the ERM estimator.
We bound each bracket term on the RHS.

Let
\{\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{N_{0}}\}\subseteq\mathcal{V}
be a minimal
\epsilon
-covering of
\mathcal{V}
in
\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1]^{D})}
-norm.
Then, there exists
j_{*}\in\{1,\ldots,N_{0}\}
such that
\|\widehat{\nu}-\nu_{j_{*}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1]^{D})}\leq\epsilon
.
A simple calculation yields that

|R(\widehat{\nu})-\widehat{R}_{n}(\widehat{\nu})|\leq 2\epsilon+|R(\nu_{j_{*}})-\widehat{R}_{n}(\nu_{j_{*}})|.

(112)

For any
\nu\in\mathcal{V}
, Lemma
28
implies that

\displaystyle|\nu({\bf X}^{i})-R(\nu)|\leq D_{1}\text{\quad and \quad}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\{\nu({\bf X}^{i})-R(\nu)\right\}^{2}\right]\leq D_{1}R(\nu)\quad\forall i\in[n],

where
D_{1}=2\widetilde{C}_{14}(F^{2}\vee 1)(\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T})
and
\widetilde{C}_{14}=\widetilde{C}_{14}(D,K,\tau_{1},\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau})
is the contstant in Lemma
28
.
Lemma 2.2.9 from
van der Vaart and Wellner (1996)
, which is standard Bernstein’s inequality for bounded random variables, implies that for any
r\geq 0
and
\nu\in\mathcal{V}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|R(\nu)-\widehat{R}_{n}(\nu)\right|\geq\frac{1}{n}\left(\frac{2D_{1}r}{3}+\sqrt{2nD_{1}R(\nu)r}\right)\right)\leq 2e^{-r}

because for any
M,v,t,x\geq 0
with
x\geq 2Mt/3+\sqrt{2vt}
,
x^{2}/(2v+2Mx/3)\geq t
.
For any
\delta>0
,

\sqrt{2D_{1}R(\nu)r/n}\leq\delta D_{1}R(\nu)+r/(n\delta)

because
\sqrt{2D_{1}R(\nu)r/n}=\sqrt{2\{\delta D_{1}R(\nu)\}\{r/(n\delta)\}}
and
\sqrt{2xy}\leq x+y
for any
x,y\geq 0
.
Combining with the last two displays, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|R(\nu)-\widehat{R}_{n}(\nu)\right|\geq\delta D_{1}R(\nu)+\frac{r}{n}\left(\frac{2D_{1}}{3}+\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\right)\leq 2e^{-r}

for any
\delta,r>0
and
\nu\in\mathcal{V}
.
The union bound implies that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|R(\nu_{j})-\widehat{R}_{n}(\nu_{j})\right|\geq\delta D_{1}R(\nu_{j})+\frac{r+\log N_{0}}{n}\left(\frac{2D_{1}}{3}+\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\quad\text{for some }j\in[N_{0}]\right)\leq 2e^{-r}

and moreover,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|R(\nu_{j})-\widehat{R}_{n}(\nu_{j})\right|\leq\delta D_{1}R(\nu_{j})+\frac{r+\log N_{0}}{n}\left(\frac{2D_{1}}{3}+\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\quad\text{for all }j\in[N_{0}]\right)\geq 1-2e^{-r}

for any
\delta>0
and
r>0
.
Then,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[|R(\nu_{j_{*}})-\widehat{R}_{n}(\nu_{j_{*}})|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\delta D_{1}{\mathbb{E}}[R(\nu_{j_{*}})]+\frac{r+\log N_{0}}{n}\left(\frac{2D_{1}}{3}+\frac{1}{\delta}\right)+2D_{1}e^{-r}

\displaystyle\leq\delta D_{1}\epsilon+\delta D_{1}{\mathbb{E}}[R(\widehat{\nu})]+\frac{r+\log N_{0}}{n}\left(\frac{2D_{1}}{3}+\frac{1}{\delta}\right)+2D_{1}e^{-r}

for any
\delta>0
and
r>0
.
With
\delta=1/(2D_{1})
and
r=2\log(2n)
, simple calculation yields that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[|R(\nu_{j_{*}})-\widehat{R}_{n}(\nu_{j_{*}})|\right]\leq\frac{{\mathbb{E}}[R(\widehat{\nu})]}{2}+\frac{8D_{1}(2\log 2+2\log n+\log N_{0})}{3n}+\frac{D_{1}}{2n^{2}}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

Combining with (
112
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[|R(\widehat{\nu})-\widehat{R}_{n}(\widehat{\nu})|\right]\leq\frac{{\mathbb{E}}[R(\widehat{\nu})]}{2}+\frac{8D_{1}(2\log(2n)+\log N_{0})}{3n}+\frac{D_{1}}{2n^{2}}+\frac{5\epsilon}{2}.

Using the same computation as above for
{\mathbb{E}}\left[|R(\widetilde{\nu})-\widehat{R}_{n}(\widetilde{\nu})|\right]
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[|R(\widetilde{\nu})-\widehat{R}_{n}(\widetilde{\nu})|\right]\leq\frac{R(\widetilde{\nu})}{2}+\frac{8D_{1}(2\log(2n)+\log N_{0})}{3n}+\frac{D_{1}}{2n^{2}}+\frac{5\epsilon}{2}.

Combining (
111
) with the last two displays, a simple calculation yields that

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbb{E}}[R(\widehat{\nu})]}{2}\leq\frac{3R(\widetilde{\nu})}{2}+\frac{16D_{1}(2\log(2n)+\log N_{0})}{3n}+\frac{D_{1}}{n^{2}}+5\epsilon

and moreover,

\begin{split}&\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\widehat{\bf f}({\bf X}_{t},t)-{\bf f}_{0}({\bf X}_{t},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]{\rm d}t\\
&\leq 3\inf_{{\bf f}\in\mathcal{F}}\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\bf f}({\bf X}_{t},t)-{\bf f}_{0}({\bf X}_{t},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]{\rm d}t+\frac{32D_{1}(2\log(2n)+\log N_{0})}{3n}+\frac{2D_{1}}{n^{2}}+10\epsilon,\end{split}

(113)

where
\widehat{\nu}=\nu_{\widehat{\bf f}}
for some
\widehat{\bf f}\in\mathcal{F}
.

Let
\widetilde{\epsilon}>0
, which will be specified later and
C=(1+2\sqrt{\log(1/\widetilde{\epsilon})})\vee\overline{T}
.
Consider functions
{\bf f}_{1},{\bf f}_{2}\in\mathcal{F}
such that
\|{\bf f}_{1}(\cdot)-{\bf f}_{2}(\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}([-C,C]^{D+1})}\leq\widetilde{\epsilon}
.
Then,

\begin{split}&\left|\nu_{{\bf f}_{1}}({\bf x})-\nu_{{\bf f}_{2}}({\bf x})\right|=\left|\ell_{{\bf f}_{1}}({\bf x})-\ell_{{\bf f}_{2}}({\bf x})\right|\\
&=\left|{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\left\|{\bf f}_{1}(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)+\frac{{\bf Z}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|{\bf f}_{2}(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf z},t)+\frac{{\bf Z}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}{\rm d}t\right]\right|\\
&=\left|{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\left\{({\bf f}_{1}-{\bf f}_{2})(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)\right\}^{\top}\left\{({\bf f}_{1}+{\bf f}_{2})(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)+\frac{2{\bf Z}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\}{\rm d}t\right]\right|\\
&\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sqrt{\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\left\|({\bf f}_{1}-{\bf f}_{2})(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}{\rm d}t}\sqrt{\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\left\|({\bf f}_{1}+{\bf f}_{2})(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)+\frac{2{\bf Z}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}{\rm d}t}\right]\\
&\leq\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\left\|({\bf f}_{1}-{\bf f}_{2})(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}{\rm d}t\right]}\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\left\|({\bf f}_{1}+{\bf f}_{2})(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)+\frac{2{\bf Z}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|_{2}^{2}{\rm d}t\right]}\\
&\leq\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\left\|({\bf f}_{1}-{\bf f}_{2})(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}{\rm d}t\right]}\sqrt{2\ell_{{\bf f}_{1}}({\bf x})+2\ell_{{\bf f}_{2}}({\bf x})},\end{split}

(114)

where the first and second inequalities hold by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
Since
\|{\bf f}_{1}({\bf x},t)-{\bf f}_{2}({\bf x},t)\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\|{\bf f}_{1}({\bf x},t)\|_{2}^{2}+2\|{\bf f}_{2}({\bf x},t)\|_{2}^{2}\leq 4DF^{2}\sigma_{t}^{-2}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|({\bf f}_{1}-{\bf f}_{2})(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq D\widetilde{\epsilon}^{2}+4DF^{2}\sigma_{t}^{-2}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\|\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z}\|_{\infty}\geq C\right)

for any
t\in[\underline{T},\overline{T}]
.
Let
{\bf Z}=(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{D})
and
{\bf x}=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{D})
.
Simple calculation yields that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\|\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z}\|_{\infty}\geq C\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{D}{\mathbb{P}}\left(|\mu_{t}x_{i}+\sigma_{t}Z_{i}|\geq C\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{D}\left\{{\mathbb{P}}\left(Z_{i}\geq\sigma_{t}^{-1}(C-\mu_{t}x_{i})\right)+{\mathbb{P}}\left(Z_{i}\leq-\sigma_{t}^{-1}(C+\mu_{t}x_{i})\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{D}{\mathbb{P}}\left(Z_{i}\geq 2\sqrt{\log(1/\widetilde{\epsilon})}\right),

where the last inequality holds because
0<\sigma_{t}\leq 1
and
\mu_{t}\in[-1,1]
.
Combining with the tail probability of the standard normal distribution, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\|\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z}\|_{\infty}\geq C\right)\leq 2D\widetilde{\epsilon}^{2}.

Since
\sigma_{t}\geq\sqrt{\underline{\tau}t}
for any
t\geq 0
, we have

\begin{split}&\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|({\bf f}_{1}-{\bf f}_{2})(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]{\rm d}t\leq\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}D\widetilde{\epsilon}^{2}+8D^{2}F^{2}\sigma_{t}^{-2}\widetilde{\epsilon}^{2}{\rm d}t\\
&\leq D\overline{T}\widetilde{\epsilon}^{2}+8D^{2}F^{2}\underline{\tau}^{-1}\widetilde{\epsilon}^{2}(\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T})\leq D_{2}\left\{\overline{T}+F^{2}(\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T})\right\}\widetilde{\epsilon}^{2},\end{split}

(115)

where
D_{2}=D_{2}(D,\underline{\tau})
.
For any
{\bf x}\in[-1,1]^{D}
and
{\bf f}\in\mathcal{F}
, we have

\displaystyle\ell_{{\bf f}}({\bf x})\leq\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}2{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\bf f}(\mu_{t}{\bf x}+\sigma_{t}{\bf Z},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]+\frac{2{\mathbb{E}}[\|{\bf Z}\|_{2}^{2}]}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\rm d}t\leq\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\frac{2DF^{2}+2D}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\rm d}t,

where the last inequality holds because
\|{\bf f}({\bf x},t)\|_{\infty}\leq F\sigma_{t}^{-1}
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle\|\ell_{{\bf f}}(\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1]^{D})}\leq\underline{\tau}^{-1}2D(F^{2}+1)(\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T}).

Combining (
114
) and (
115
) with the last display, we have

\displaystyle\left|\nu_{{\bf f}_{1}}({\bf x})-\nu_{{\bf f}_{2}}({\bf x})\right|\leq\sqrt{4D_{2}D\underline{\tau}^{-1}\left\{\overline{T}+F^{2}(\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T})\right\}(F^{2}+1)(\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T})\widetilde{\epsilon}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq D_{3}(F+1)^{2}\left(\sqrt{\overline{T}}+\sqrt{\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T}}\right)\left(\sqrt{\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T}}\right)\widetilde{\epsilon},

where
D_{3}=D_{3}(D_{2},D,\underline{\tau})
.
Let
\epsilon=D_{3}(F+1)^{2}(\sqrt{\overline{T}}+\sqrt{\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T}})(\sqrt{\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T}})n^{-2}
.
Then,

\displaystyle N\left(\epsilon,\mathcal{V},\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1]^{D})}\right)\leq N\left(n^{-2},\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}([-C,C]^{D+1})}\right).

Combining with (
113
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\widehat{\bf f}({\bf X}_{t},t)-{\bf f}_{0}({\bf X}_{t},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq 3\inf_{{\bf f}\in\mathcal{F}}\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\bf f}({\bf X}_{t},t)-{\bf f}_{0}({\bf X}_{t},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{32D_{1}\left\{2\log(2n)+\log N\left(n^{-2},\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}([-C,C]^{D+1})}\right)\right\}}{3n}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{D_{1}+10D_{3}(F+1)^{2}\left(\sqrt{\overline{T}}+\sqrt{\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T}}\right)\left(\sqrt{\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T}}\right)}{n^{2}}.

Since
D_{1}=2\widetilde{C}_{14}(F^{2}\vee 1)(\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T})
, there exists a constant
D_{4}=D_{4}(\widetilde{C}_{14},D_{3})
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\widehat{\bf f}({\bf X}_{t},t)-{\bf f}_{0}({\bf X}_{t},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq 3\inf_{{\bf f}\in\mathcal{F}}\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\bf f}({\bf X}_{t},t)-{\bf f}_{0}({\bf X}_{t},t)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{D_{5}\left\{\log(2n)+\log N\left(n^{-2},\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}([-C,C]^{D+1})}\right)\right\}}{n},

where

\displaystyle D_{5}=D_{4}(F^{2}\vee 1)\left(\sqrt{\overline{T}}+\sqrt{\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T}}\right)\left(\sqrt{\log\overline{T}-\log\underline{T}}\right).

The assertion follows by redefining the constants.
∎

C.5
Proof of Theorem
3

In this subsection, we provide the proof of Theorem
3
with auxiliary lemmas.
For two probability measures
P
and
Q
on
\mathcal{X}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{D}
, the Kullback-Leibler (KL) divergence is defined as

\displaystyle\text{KL}(P,Q)=\begin{cases}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\log\frac{{\rm d}P}{{\rm d}Q}{\rm d}P,&\text{if }P\ll Q\\
\infty,&\text{else}.\end{cases}

We often denote
\text{KL}(P,Q)
as
\text{KL}(p,q)
, where
p
and
q
are densities of
P
and
Q
, respectively.
Hereafter,
C=C({\rm all})
means that
C
is a constant depending on
(\beta,d,D,K,\tau_{\rm min},\tau_{\rm max},\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau})
.

Proof of Theorem
3
.

Let
\widetilde{\tau}_{\rm min}=\tau_{\rm min}(2\beta+d)/d
and
\widetilde{\tau}_{\rm max}=\tau_{\rm max}(2\beta+d)/d
.
Let
C_{3},C_{4}
be the constants in Theorem
5
depending on
(\beta,d,D,K,\widetilde{\tau}_{\rm min},\widetilde{\tau}_{\rm max},\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau})
.
Then, by replacing
m
in Theorem
5
with
n^{d/(2\beta+d)}
,
\underline{T}
with
m^{-\widetilde{\tau}_{\rm min}}
, and
\overline{T}
with
\widetilde{\tau}_{\rm max}\log m
, there exists a class of permutation matrices
\mathcal{P}_{{\bf m}}
and a class of weight-sharing neural networks
\mathcal{F}_{\rm WSNN}=\mathcal{F}_{\rm WSNN}(L,{\bf d},s,M,\mathcal{P}_{{\bf m}})
with

\displaystyle L\leq D_{1}(\log n)^{6}\log\log n,\quad\|{\bf d}\|_{\infty}\leq D_{1}n^{\frac{d(D+1)}{2\beta+d}},

\displaystyle s\leq D_{1}n^{\frac{d}{2\beta+d}}(\log n)^{5}\log\log n,\quad M\leq\exp(D_{1}\{\log n\}^{6}),

\displaystyle\|{\bf m}\|_{\infty}\leq D_{1}n^{\frac{dD}{2\beta+d}}

satisfying

\inf_{{\bf f}\in\mathcal{F}_{\rm WSNN}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\infty}}\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\left\|{\bf f}({\bf x},t)-\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x})\right\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bf x})\ {\rm d}{\bf x}{\rm d}t\leq D_{1}n^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}}(\log n)^{4D+4\beta+1},

(116)

for every
n\geq C_{4}^{(2\beta+d)/d}
, where
\underline{T}=n^{-\tau_{\rm min}},\overline{T}=\tau_{\rm max}\log n,D_{1}=D_{1}(\beta,d,D,C_{3})
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\infty}=\left\{\|{\bf f}(\cdot,t)\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{D})}\leq D_{1}\sigma_{t}^{-1}\sqrt{\log n}\quad\forall t\in[\underline{T},\overline{T}]\right\}.

Let
\widehat{\bf f}
be an empirical risk minimizer over the class
\mathcal{F}_{\rm WSNN}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\infty}
, defined as in (
6
).
By the Triangle inequality, we have

{\mathbb{E}}\left[d_{\rm TV}(P_{0},\widehat{P}_{\underline{T}})\right]\leq d_{\rm TV}(P_{0},P_{\underline{T}})+{\mathbb{E}}\left[d_{\rm TV}(P_{\underline{T}},\widehat{P}_{\underline{T}})\right].

(117)

We proceed to control each term on the RHS separately.

Let
\widetilde{C}_{12}=\widetilde{C}_{12}(\beta,D,K,\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau})
and
\widetilde{C}_{13}(\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau})
be the constants in Lemma
26
.
Let
D_{2}=D_{2}(\beta,d,\widetilde{C}_{13},C_{4})
be a positive constant such that
D_{2}\geq C_{4}^{(2\beta+d)/d}
and
\underline{T}=n^{-\tau_{\rm min}}\leq\widetilde{C}_{13}
for every
n\geq D_{2}
.
Then, Lemma
26
implies that

\displaystyle d_{\rm TV}(P_{0},P_{\underline{T}})=\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\left|p_{0}({\bf x})-p_{\underline{T}}({\bf x})\right|{\rm d}{\bf x}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-1}\widetilde{C}_{12}\left\{\underline{T}\log(1/\underline{T})\right\}^{\frac{\beta\wedge 1}{2}}=2^{-1}\widetilde{C}_{12}\tau_{\rm min}^{(\beta\wedge 1)/2}n^{-\frac{\tau_{\rm min}(\beta\wedge 1)}{2}}(\log n)^{\frac{\beta\wedge 1}{2}},

for every
n\geq D_{2}
.
Since
\tau_{\rm min}\geq\frac{2\beta}{(2\beta+d)(\beta\wedge 1)}
, the last display is bounded by

2^{-1}\widetilde{C}_{12}\tau_{\rm min}^{(\beta\wedge 1)/2}n^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}(\log n)^{\frac{\beta\wedge 1}{2}}.

(118)

Consider functions
q_{1},q_{2}:{\mathbb{R}}^{D}\times[0,\overline{T}-\underline{T}]\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
such that
q_{1}({\bf x},t)=p_{\overline{T}-t}({\bf x})
and
q_{2}({\bf x},t)=\widehat{p}_{\overline{T}-t}({\bf x})
.
Then, each
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
satisfy the corresponding well-known Fokker-Planck equation
(
Le Bris and Lions, 2008
;
Bogachev et al., 2022
;
Pavliotis, 2014
)
:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}q_{1}({\bf x},t)=-\sum_{i=1}^{D}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\left[{\bf b}_{1}({\bf x},t)q_{1}({\bf x},t)\right]+\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{j=1}^{D}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}\left[a_{1}(t)\delta_{ij}q_{1}({\bf x},t)\right]

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}q_{2}({\bf x},t)=-\sum_{i=1}^{D}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\left[{\bf b}_{2}({\bf x},t)q_{2}({\bf x},t)\right]+\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{j=1}^{D}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}\left[a_{2}(t)\delta_{ij}q_{2}({\bf x},t)\right],

where
\delta_{ij}
denotes the Kronecker delta and

\displaystyle{\bf b}_{1}({\bf x},t)=\alpha_{\overline{T}-t}{\bf x}+2\alpha_{\overline{T}-t}\nabla\log p_{\overline{T}-t}({\bf x}),\quad a_{1}(t)=\alpha_{\overline{T}-t},

\displaystyle{\bf b}_{2}({\bf x},t)=\alpha_{\overline{T}-t}{\bf x}+2\alpha_{\overline{T}-t}\widehat{\bf f}({\bf x},\overline{T}-t),\quad a_{2}(t)=\alpha_{\overline{T}-t}.

Following the Remark 2.3 of
Bogachev et al. (2016)
, we have

\begin{split}&d_{\rm TV}(P_{\underline{T}},\widehat{P}_{\underline{T}})=d_{\rm TV}\left(q_{1}(\cdot,\overline{T}-\underline{T}),q_{2}(\cdot,\overline{T}-\underline{T})\right)\\
&\leq d_{\rm TV}\left(q_{1}(\cdot,0),q_{2}(\cdot,0)\right)+\left(\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}4\alpha_{t}\left\|\widehat{\bf f}({\bf x},t)-\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf x})\right\|_{2}^{2}p_{t}({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}{\rm d}t\right)^{1/2}.\end{split}

(119)

Recall that
\widehat{p}_{\overline{T}}=\phi_{1}
. By Pinsker’s inequality, we have

d_{\rm TV}\left(q_{1}(\cdot,0),q_{2}(\cdot,0)\right)=d_{\rm TV}\left(p_{\overline{T}},\phi_{1}\right)\leq\sqrt{\text{KL}\left(p_{\overline{T}},\phi_{1}\right)/2}.

(120)

Since KL divergence is convex in its first argument, Jensen’s inequality implies that

\displaystyle\text{KL}\left(p_{\overline{T}},\phi_{1}\right)\leq\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\text{KL}\left(\mathcal{N}(\mu_{\overline{T}}{\bf y},\sigma_{\overline{T}}{\mathbb{I}}_{D}),\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{0}_{D},{\mathbb{I}}_{D})\right){\rm d}P_{0}({\bf y}).

because
p_{\overline{T}}(\cdot)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\phi_{\sigma_{\overline{T}}}(\cdot-\mu_{\overline{T}}{\bf y}){\rm d}P_{0}({\bf y})
.
KL divergence between two
D
-dimensional Gaussian random variables is known as

\displaystyle\text{KL}\left(\mathcal{N}(\mu_{1},\Sigma_{1}),\mathcal{N}(\mu_{2},\Sigma_{2})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left[\log\left(\frac{\det(\Sigma_{2})}{\det(\Sigma_{1})}\right)-D+(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})^{\top}\Sigma_{2}^{-1}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})+{\rm tr}\left(\Sigma_{2}^{-1}\Sigma_{1}\right)\right].

Using that, we have

\displaystyle\text{KL}\left(p_{\overline{T}},\phi_{1}\right)\leq\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{D}}\frac{1}{2}\left(\mu_{\overline{T}}^{2}\|{\bf y}\|_{2}^{2}+D\sigma_{\overline{T}}-D-D\log\sigma_{\overline{T}}\right){\rm d}P_{0}({\bf y})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\mu_{\overline{T}}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{P_{0}}\left[\|{\bf X}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\right]+D\sigma_{\overline{T}}-D-D\log\sigma_{\overline{T}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{D}{2}\left\{\mu_{\overline{T}}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{1-\mu_{\overline{T}}^{2}}-1\right|+\left|\log\left(1-\mu_{\overline{T}}^{2}\right)/2\right|\right\},

where the last inequality holds because
{\mathbb{E}}[\|{\bf X}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq D
and
\mu_{\overline{T}}^{2}+\sigma_{\overline{T}}^{2}=1
.
Since
\mu_{\overline{T}}\leq\exp(-\underline{\tau}\overline{T})
and
|\log(1-x)|\leq x/(1-x)
for
0<x<1
, the last display is bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{D}{2}\left\{\mu_{\overline{T}}^{2}+\frac{\mu_{\overline{T}}^{2}}{\sqrt{1-\mu_{\overline{T}}^{2}}+1}+\frac{\mu_{\overline{T}}^{2}}{2(1-\mu_{\overline{T}}^{2})}\right\}.

Note also that
\mu_{\overline{T}}^{2}\geq\exp(-2\overline{\tau}\overline{T})=n^{-2\overline{\tau}\tau_{\rm max}}
and
\mu_{\overline{T}}^{2}\leq\exp(-2\underline{\tau}\overline{T})=n^{-2\underline{\tau}\tau_{\rm max}}
by the definition.
Let
D_{3}=D_{3}(\tau_{\rm max},\overline{\tau},D_{2})>0
be a constant such that
D_{3}\geq D_{2}
and
n^{-2\overline{\tau}\tau_{\rm max}}\leq 1/2
for every
n\geq D_{3}
.
Then, the last display is further bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{5D\mu_{\overline{T}}^{2}}{4}\leq\left(\frac{5D}{4}\right)n^{-2\underline{\tau}\tau_{\rm max}}\leq\left(\frac{5D}{4}\right)n^{-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+d}},

where the last inequality holds because
\tau_{\rm max}\geq\frac{\beta}{\underline{\tau}(2\beta+d)}
.
Combining with (
120
), we have

d_{\rm TV}\left(q_{1}(\cdot,0),q_{2}(\cdot,0)\right)=d_{\rm TV}\left(p_{\overline{T}},\phi_{1}\right)\leq\sqrt{5D/8}n^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}},\quad\forall n\geq D_{3}.

(121)

Lemma
27
implies that there exists a constant
D_{4}=D_{4}(\beta,d,D,D_{1})
such that the metric entropy bound for
\mathcal{F}
follows by

\log N\left(n^{-2},\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}([-D_{5},D_{5}]^{D+1})}\right)\leq D_{4}n^{\frac{d}{2\beta+d}}(\log n)^{17}(\log\log n)^{2},

(122)

where
D_{5}=(1+2\sqrt{2\log n})\vee(\tau_{\rm max}\log n)
.
Let
\widetilde{C}_{14}=\widetilde{C}_{14}(D,K,\tau_{1},\overline{\tau},\underline{\tau})
be the constant in Proposition
29
.
Then, by replacing
F
in Proposition
29
with
D_{1}\sqrt{\log n}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\widehat{\bf f}({\bf X}_{t},t)-\nabla\log p_{t}(X_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq 3\inf_{{\bf f}\in\mathcal{F}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\infty}}\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|{\bf f}({\bf X}_{t},t)-\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf X}_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{D_{6}(\log n)^{2}}{n}\left\{\log N\left(n^{-2},\mathcal{F}\cap\mathcal{F}_{\infty},\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}([-D_{5},D_{5}]^{D+1})}\right)+\log(2n)\right\},

where
D_{6}=D_{6}(\tau_{\rm max},\tau_{\rm min},D_{1},\widetilde{C}_{14})
.
Combining (
116
) and (
122
) with the last display, Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\{\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}\left\|\widehat{\bf f}({\bf X}_{t},t)-\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf X}_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}{\rm d}t\right\}^{1/2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\int_{\underline{T}}^{\overline{T}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\widehat{\bf f}({\bf X}_{t},t)-\nabla\log p_{t}({\bf X}_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]{\rm d}t\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq D_{7}n^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}\left\{(\log n)^{2D+2\beta+1}+(\log n)^{10}\right\},\quad\forall n\geq D_{3},

where the first inequality holds by Jensen’s inequality and
D_{7}=D_{7}(D_{1},D_{4},D_{6})
.
Since
\alpha_{t}\leq\overline{\tau}
for all
t\in[\underline{T},\overline{T}]
, combining (
119
) and (
121
) with the last display implies that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[d_{\rm TV}\left(P_{\underline{T}},\widehat{P}_{\underline{T}}\right)\right]\leq D_{8}n^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}\left\{(\log n)^{2D+2\beta+1}+(\log n)^{10}\right\},\quad\forall n\geq D_{3}

where
D_{8}=D_{8}(D,\overline{\tau},D_{6})

Combining (
117
) and (
118
) with the last display, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[d_{\rm TV}\left(P_{0},\widehat{P}_{\underline{T}}\right)\right]\leq D_{9}n^{-\frac{\beta}{2\beta+d}}\left\{(\log n)^{2D+2\beta+1}+(\log n)^{10}\right\},\quad\forall n\geq D_{3},

where
D_{9}=D_{9}(\beta,d,\widetilde{C}_{12},D_{7})
.
The assertion follows by re-defining the constants.
